## D-76 — The `landing` seat is DERIVED, not stored, so the family has ONE seat and the entry's id can be spelled

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** the user's decision D-75 carries — the landing entry's id is `'landing'` —
plus D-75's own four measurements, re-measured on this head before the change and again after it (2026-11-02).

**Decision:** `['tabs','landing']` is **the landing ENTRY's own leaf and no longer a SEAT.** `composeRecord` writes the `order`
array and one member per seated id and **no witness at all**; `landingOf` derives the witness — the landing entry's row when it
reads active, else `order`'s active row — and `read()`'s `landing` member **is that derivation**. One namespace is left where
there were two, which is what frees `landing` to be an entry's id. **The five pairs are unmoved**, and that is the test of the
change rather than a hope: `exactly-one-active` still requires one active among the survivors, the empty sequence still re-mints
the landing entry, and `tab-has-content`'s fallback is still the authored `'tabs-landing-page'` CONTENT target.

- **Why, in the collision this head measured** (a scratch profile with the five declarations batched, one fresh instance per arm).
  The family keys one member per tab in the SAME flat object as its seats, so with the entry spelled `'landing'` the composer wrote
  the row and then the seat STRING over it: the construction instant's own bytes read `{"landing":"landing","order":["landing"]}`
  and `read()` answered `rows: []`. **A HEALTHY record was worse** — the same `commit({id:'landing',…})` was ANSWERED
  `{status:'committed'}` and wrote `{"landing":"landing","order":["tab-1","landing"], …}` with **no `landing` row member**, so the
  record answered `committed` over a read that names a landing row it does not hold. **And a record carrying the landing ROW at
  that key, no string seat, passes all five pairs** and reads `landing: 'tab-1'`: the seat, not the id, was the whole obstruction.
- **Measured after it, the same arms on this head.** The construction write's own bytes are now
  `{"order":["tabs-landing-page"],"tabs-landing-page":{…}}` with `read()` answering `landing: 'tabs-landing-page'`, and the same
  `commit({id:'landing',…})` on a healthy record writes a real row member at that key, seats it in `order` and reads it back — so
  the hole was the seat and it is closed by the derivation, not by a guard over it.
- **What this means for a client**: `read()`'s `landing` member is the DERIVATION, not a stored pointer — `'landing'` when the
  landing entry is the shown tab, `''` on a record no legal act has left — so a client reads it as "which tab is the terminal" and
  never composes it. The rows are the state; this is the one member of them that names the shown one.
- **Two steps follow this row and each is appended here as it lands**: the refusal of an entry id equal to the one remaining
  stored seat (`order`), and then the id move itself. **Neither is taken on this row's word**: each is measured on its own head.
- **⟶ THE SEAT-NAME GUARD, appended when it landed (2026-11-02).** After the derivation `order` is the ONE stored seat, so an entry
  id of `order` is unrepresentable exactly as `landing` was — and it was accepted as silently: measured with the guard removed,
  `commit({id:'order',…})` was ANSWERED `{"status":"committed","repaired":true}` and the record **lost every tab** (the member write
  overwrote the `order` array the whole family is seated from), while `remove('order')` was answered `committed` as the store's own
  success over a name that is no member. **Both routes that take an id refuse it before any store act**, under `SEAT_NAME_REASON`
  (`'seat-name'`) and `SEAT_NAME_MESSAGE`, and one crossing row reads the refusal at both entry points and the record byte-identical after.
- **⟶ THE ID MOVED, appended when it landed (2026-11-02).** `TABS_LANDING_ID` is `'landing'` — `LANDING_ENTRY_KEY`, the literal
  `TABS_LANDING_ADDRESS` is spelled from — and EXPORTED, because the renderer pass reads one spelling. `landingRow`, `mintRecord`,
  `healRecord`, the crossing's rows, L1 §1 and its two `§5.U` posts, L2 §1/§2/§6 and L3 design §2 carry it; `TAB_CONTENT_FALLBACK`
  did NOT move, `'landing'` naming no authored node. **`D-75`'s "held" clause is superseded here; its dated measurements stay
  written as that head's own** (D-46). Measured: `order: ['landing']`, `read().landing` = `'landing'`, a commit under that id
  updates ONE member in place, and `remove` of it as the last tab re-mints it.

## D-77 — The renderer's tab client APPEARS in Stage A: one read-only module, one un-awaited boot read at the END of `main()`, and an absent member that answers

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, verbatim — "Stage A of the
renderer migration — the client APPEARS, nothing is deleted" — on the staging already agreed in the corpus
(`strata/next-steps.md` row 1; D-63's client role, `docs/L2/store-tabs-record/interface.md` §6).

**Decision:** the renderer gets the tab record's client as ONE module, `src/renderer/tabs-client.ts` (claimed by this
record's map row), and **this stage is ADDITIVE**: `TABS_CONSTRAINT`, the graph-store record, `readTabsRef`, `mintTabId`,
`closeTab`, `evaluateTabsBootStep`, `driveTabsLandingPage`, the `file.tabs.*` writes and the Y-1/Y-2/Y-3 hand-off are
unmoved, and no element, route or answer changes. The client reads through `tabs.read()`, re-reads after every
`tabs.onChanged` delivery, holds no record of its own, and takes **no write route at all** — `commit` and `remove` are
Stage B's, and a write site with no caller is a route nobody drives.

- **WHERE THE BOOT READ SITS, AND WHY IT IS NOT AWAITED.** `main()`'s boot order is a GATE's reading:
  `tests/e2e/bundle-boot-seam/` asserts the bundle-boot trace's ORDER (hand-off → store → hydrate → boot step → runtime →
  pages → gutters → Y-3 → `ready()`). The instrument's one read is therefore STARTED as the LAST statement of `main()`,
  **after `bridge.ready()`**, and its promise is not awaited: no step's order moves, and a bridge that is slow, silent or
  failing cannot delay the first graph load, the pages' drive or the ready signal. **A pass that needs the reading EARLIER
  is changing the boot, and that is Stage B's own measurement — never a silent move of this call site.**
- **WHAT THE ABSENT MEMBER MEANS, STATED.** `window.provident.tabs` is optional at its declaration and is simply NOT THERE
  in three realms: the node suite's layout-less shim, a plain page loaded without the preload, and a bridge older than the
  crossing. In all three the client answers `{ status: 'absent' }` with a closed reason (`'no-tabs-member'`, or
  `'no-read-route'` where the member carries no `read`), and `subscribe` answers `subscribed: false` with a withdrawal that
  is still callable. **It is NOT a refusal**: L2 §3's refusal vocabulary is the store's own words, and a realm with no
  crossing has no store that spoke — so a missing instrument never reads as a record that failed.
- **`unread` IS THE CLIENT'S OWN THIRD STATE AND NOT A REFUSAL.** A route that rejected, and an answer outside L2 §2's
  declaration, both answer `unread` with the divergence named, because a record that could not be read must never be
  rendered as a record that was empty. The client is TOTAL on all four arms: nothing it does throws out of a read or a
  subscription.
- **THE WITNESS IS ONE PREDICATE, DERIVED FROM `read()`'s ANSWER ALONE** (L2 §6): the landing terminal stands where
  `landing` names a row reading `active: true` AND no other row does — one comparison and not two — and the error terminal
  where a row carries an `error`; both nodes are the ENVELOPE's, resolved through `Runtime.elementForNodeId`, and the
  client authors no element and no text. The derivation is exported, and the renderer bundle re-exports it, so the live
  driver reads the SAME predicate off both records rather than a re-spelling of it.

---

## D-78 — A STORE is a `provident-storeroom` instance and there are to be TWO: the provident-ssr graph is a RENDERING ENGINE, and `next-steps` row 1 is a PURGE

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** the user's three rulings, verbatim, carried on this head's inventory pass (`928e1b1`): **(1)** "There should only ever be the two crossing instances (normal + secure). Any references to other stores, including the renderer's graph store, is a legacy code feature that is obsolete and should be purged." **(2)** "The intended renderer process is that the listener drives the data loading, while the listener is driven by the store creation load + the constraint/repair passes." **(3)** "The provident-ssr graph is not a store. It is a rendering engine. Do not refer to it as a store. Do not use it as a store. It controls the render and the data shape is driven by the listener events."

**Decision.** **A STORE is a `provident-storeroom` instance, and there are to be exactly two**: a **normal crossing** and a **secure crossing**. Everything else that persists or holds state is **LEGACY and is PURGED**, the app's own frozen graph store included. **The provident-ssr graph is a RENDERING ENGINE and is none of those things**: it is **not a store**, it is **not called one**, and it is **not used as one** — nothing is held in it as state and nothing is read out of it as data. It controls the render, and **the data shape it renders is driven by the listener events**. The chain is **store acts** (creation load, constraint/repair passes) → **listener deliveries** → **the read** → **the data shape** → **the rendering engine renders it**, in that order and no other.

**The inventory this row rules on.** **Six store-like things stand at this head, and two are intended.** Intended: **(a)** the **normal crossing** — `createProfileStore` at the boot's one construction (`src/main/main.ts:132`, the five tab-record declarations batched, `<userData>/provident-profile.json`); **(b)** the **secure crossing** — the operator-settings instance (`src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:405`, three `file` addresses on `provident-operator-settings.json`, no constraint and no listener). Legacy: **(c)** the app's own frozen graph store `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts`, constructed at `src/renderer/renderer.ts:819` — **the app's, never the package's**; **(d)** `src/renderer/store.ts`, the re-derive store, landed and imported by no file under `src/`; **(e)** `src/main/data/store.ts:120`, a **second instance of the seam opened on the boot store's own file**; **(f)** `src/main/module-store.ts`, the module registry on raw `fs`. The frozen store's `file.*` mirror is additionally persisted into the secure crossing's `['operator-app-state']` address — legacy content inside an intended store.

**`next-steps.md` row 1 is a PURGE and not a migration.** It was filed as a move ("the renderer reads and commits through `provident.tabs`"); what it carries now is deletion to a stated end, and its closure is **two instances and no other store-like thing in `src/`**.

- **The measurement that makes it a purge.** Exactly one path wires a legacy store into the render: the boot hand-off (`renderer.ts:1301`–`1339`) hydrates the frozen store from the secure instance's app-state rows, and the gutter wiring reads it (`renderer.ts:1734`, `:1880`) to clamp and to `state-slice` one authored node. **The tab record reaches no render at all** — `driveTabsLandingPage` (`renderer.ts:681`) resolves the two authored pages and its answer is **discarded** where `main()` calls it (`renderer.ts:1385`) — so ruling 2's chain is stated by this corpus and implemented by nothing today.
- **Ruling 3's sweep, run on this head BEFORE this row was filed.** `grep -rn "graph store\|graph-store\|store-core-graph" strata/ docs/ src/ AGENTS.md` answered **39 hits — 20 `strata/`, 10 `docs/`, 9 `src/`, 0 `AGENTS.md` — and not one is the rendering engine**: every hit names the app's frozen store, its map row, or a test that pins it, and no file in this tree calls the rendering engine a store. **This row adds four hits to that figure by naming the terms and quoting ruling 1**, whose own words are "the renderer's graph store" — so 39 is the reading at the instant before it landed, and the citation is that instant rather than a number a record can move by being written (D-43).
- **It supersedes the framing of the rows above it, never their dated text** (D-46). The register it reads is D-71 to D-77 — the change push returned to `['tabs']`, a repair's return was made explicit and its write made to name its commit, a boot battery's teardown was made to prove zero survivors, and the landing seat was derived and then its entry id moved; those measurements stand as written, and the package-facing rows among them stay the package's (`D-5`, `D-52`).

`docs/L2/store-tabs-record/interface.md`, `docs/L2/security-store-storeroom/persistence.md` and `docs/L2/central-data/persistence.md` each name the module DELETED rather than parked.

**⟶ APPENDED 2026-11-02 — ITEM (f) IS MEASURED AND THIS STAGE STOPS ON IT: THE ROWS WIN THE AUTHORITY, THE JSON IS A ONE-WAY MIGRATION INPUT, AND THE ARM THAT READS IT IS NOT DEAD — SO NOTHING IS DELETED.**
**The stage as landed is a measurement and a REFUSAL: `src/main/module-store.ts` (207 lines), `tests/module-store.test.ts` (215 lines, 12 specs, on notice), `main.ts:197`'s `legacyModulesPath`, `:226`'s `createModuleStore({ path: legacyModulesPath })`, `:227`'s `.list()` and `:237`'s `rmSync` all STAND — 0 lines deleted, 0 files moved, 0 doc re-grained.**

**SO THE ROWS ARE THE AUTHORITY OF THE PRODUCT'S WHOLE LIFE AND THE JSON'S WRITERS ARE DEAD**: `src/` holds ONE construction (`main.ts:226`) and ONE call on it (`.list()`, `:227`) — no site under `src/` invokes `put`/`remove`/`setDisabled` — and the registry the app serves and mutates is the `ModuleStore` object literal at `main.ts:257-321`, whose every write is ONE `settingsSurface.commit` of the whole rows list (`:305`, `:312`, `:319`), read back through `:246-282`, `syncModuleRouter` (`:326`), the server's `moduleStore` option (`:335`), `IPC_MODULE_GET` (`:467`) and Y-1 (`:355-359`).
**AND THE ARM IS NOT DEAD, WHICH IS WHY THIS STAGE DELETES NOTHING.** It is reachable on EVERY boot, guarded only by the file's own existence; its `rmSync` is what makes it run once; and it is the ONLY path by which a registry written before the re-point at `3673013` reaches the product — deleting it drops module state for any profile that has not booted since, and **no row sanctions that loss**: D-75's *"no migration, no users"* ruling is taken for the TABS rows in the operator-settings carrier, on the ground that no install holds them, and it names this registry nowhere.
**AND IT IS NOT D-78'S FIRST DELETION IN KIND, WHICH THE CONTRAST MEASURES**: `src/renderer/store.ts` left all five artifacts BYTE-IDENTICAL, while this store is LIVE IN THE BUNDLE — `grep` answers `module put: record must be an object` twice and `provident-modules.json` once in `dist/main/main.mjs` (`1,364,330` bytes) and 0 in `battery-host.mjs` and `standalone.mjs`. A deletion here MOVES an artifact and a boot's behaviour, so there is no "dead in the tree and in every built artifact" proving to make.
**THE CENSUS IS UNMOVED BECAUSE NOTHING MOVED, AND WHAT IT WOULD HAVE OWED IS STATED SO THE NEXT PASS NEED NOT RE-DERIVE IT**: no `tests/e2e/` file names the store, its file or its call (`0` hits), so no gating row moves; `S-10` bootstraps no `main.ts` and therefore never sees the name — it pins three names in a directory it seeds and exactly two written ones *after excluding the retired carrier*; `store-compliance-live.mjs`'s `DECLARED_WRITTEN_JSON` (two) and `DECLARED_SEEDED_RETIRED_JSON` (two) declare it nowhere, so it is a THIRD name held only by `tests/store-security.test.ts:572` (`main.ts`'s one userData literal, `toEqual(['provident-modules.json'])`) and by three on-notice witness lists (`scripts/electron-ui.mjs:663`, `ui-leg-seam:58,445,484`, `ui-leg-contract:3826`); a sanctioned purge would re-derive the first to `toEqual([])` and never delete it, and `store-modules-bytes`/`store-modules-seams` name the FROZEN GRAPH STORE, not this registry, and are untouched.
**OWED AND NOT TAKEN HERE: a ruling on whether the module registry's legacy file gets the disposition the tabs rows got.** Until it is given, `next-steps.md` row 1's remaining stage stands open and `pending.md` row 5 carries the wait; the frozen graph store (`src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts`) is the purge's only remaining DELETION, and the line above numbering `src/main/module-store.ts` stage *(iii)* is re-read here as this same stage, not a fourth.

## D-79 — The THIRD instance is COLLAPSED onto the normal crossing: two instances over one file silently discard each other's writes, and `data.*` at `['tabs']` is now REPAIRED while it answers `committed`

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** D-78's ruling that there are to be exactly two `provident-storeroom` instances, and this head's inventory, which named `src/main/data/store.ts`'s construction as a **second instance of the seam opened on the file the normal crossing already owns** — the same `provident-profile.json` the boot opens at `src/main/main.ts:132`.

**Decision.** **The surplus instance is collapsed and the app opens its profile once.** `createDataSurface` takes the caller's handle (`DataSurfaceOptions.store`) and `src/main/main.ts:297` passes the boot's own `centralStore`, which exists twelve lines earlier; the surface's port returns that instance and **constructs nothing**, so `data.*` acts through the normal crossing — the instance carrying the five tab-record declarations — and one register stands over the one file. **The handle is OPTIONAL and the absent arm is the batteries', not the app's**: an operation whose reading answers `absent` answers the cold tier without constructing anything, so a battery stands the surface alone on a seeded directory; a **supplied** store would create the file (a construction creates its `filePath` exclusively), the reading would answer `occupied`, and gating rows that pin the cold tier (`tests/e2e/central-data/scenarios.test.ts:134-135`, `:276`, `:385`, `:405-406`) could not stand. **The two-instance census is now INSTANCES, not importers** — `S-9` counts importing FILES and was blind to this instance because the surface reached the package through the seam, so it stayed green while a second store opened the same bytes; its assertions are untouched and still pin three importers (the seam, the operator-settings instance, the tab-record instance), and its TITLE, which read "exactly two" against an assertion naming three since the tab record landed its own import, is corrected to three.

- **THE HAZARD, MEASURED BEFORE THE CHANGE, AND IT IS NOT BENIGN.** A throwaway harness on a scratch profile, deleted after, opened two `createProfileStore`s over one name. *A constructed, B constructed on the empty file, A wrote `{"boot":"A-row"}`, B wrote `{"dataRow":"B-row"}`* → the file held **only B's row**; A's gone. *A wrote `{"seed":"A-seed"}`, B constructed and ADOPTED those bytes, B wrote `fromB`, A wrote `fromA`* → the file came back `{"fromA":"A-row","seed":"A-seed"}`, **B's row discarded**. *Both wrote `['x']`, A then B* → the disk held B, **A answered `"A"` and B answered `"B"`**, and neither call threw or refused. **The instance that writes last flushes its OWN whole register**, so the loss is silent in both directions and visible only from the instance that did not flush. The two 4096-entry journals are IN-MEMORY and never reach the file (8 writes → 57 bytes, a flat `{"k0":0,…}` map), and a fresh instance adopts whatever stands — so what interleaves is the register, and D-78's "two independent journals, one file" is the mechanism, not the visible bytes.
- **AND THE SURPLUS INSTANCE COULD LEAVE A TAB RECORD THAT NO CONSTRAINT PASS REPAIRS.** An instance with no constraints — exactly what the surface built, the seam's defaults being `listeners: []`, `constraints: []` — accepted `write(['tabs'], 'file', 42)` and `['tabs']` read back **`42`**; the boot instance went on reading its own legal record in memory, and **the next boot repaired nothing**: a constrained construction over that file answered `read(['tabs']) = 42`. So the third instance was not merely extra: it was a second writer of the tab record's file, outside the declarations that police it, and it could leave a record the crossing then serves as a tab.
- **THE JOURNAL LENGTH IS PRESERVED EXACTLY, AND THE LAZY CONSTRUCTION SAVED NO I/O.** Both sides asked for `4096` — `main.ts` passes no `journalLength` to either, so `DEFAULT_JOURNAL_LENGTH` (`src/main/storeroom.ts:71`) is the figure on both — and the supplied instance's journal IS the one the surface asked for; a caller that supplies a differently-sized instance fixes the surface's figure too, because **a store's journal is set at creation and never moves**, so there is nothing to preserve there and no way to. The deferral bought nothing at the file: the boot store had already opened and created the file at `:132`, so what waited was a **second register and a second journal in memory**, and the collapse deletes exactly that.
- **WHAT A `data.*` WRITE AT A POLICED ADDRESS NOW ANSWERS, MEASURED THROUGH THIS SURFACE WITH THE BOOT'S HANDLE.** `write({path:['tabs'], value:42})` → **`{"status":"committed","path":["tabs"],"cursor":3}`**, and what STANDS at `['tabs']` is the legal landing-seated record — the five declarations all fired and repaired it. `{}`, a record naming two actives, and a record naming none answer the same and stand the same. `delete(['tabs'])` → `{"status":"committed","removed":false,"tiers":[],"cursor":null}`, the record intact. **So it is PROTECTION of the record and an UNTRUTHFUL `committed`** — `committed` is this surface's word for a write that landed, and the write landed and was then rewritten inside its own commit. At an address the declarations do not NAME, `write(['tabs','rows'], 'v')` is accepted and the member stands in the store until the record's next commit composes the record whole and drops it, while the record's own `read()` ignores it throughout (measured both ways). **A GUARD IS PROPOSED, NOT BUILT**: the store's write receipt carries `triggered`, the surface discards it, and answering `committed` with the repairs named — the tab record's own `repaired` member, derived the same way — would make the answer honest; that moves L2 §2/§3's closed vocabulary and needs a ruling.
- **WHAT DID NOT MOVE, PROVED BY RUNNING THE EXISTING SUITES UNEDITED.** The seven on-notice central-data files (3 integration + 4 unit) are green at `59` rows with **not one line changed**, and the gating battery is `12/12`: `read`, `write` and `delete` at an ordinary path answer exactly what they answered before, and the cold tier still constructs nothing. **One answer WIDENS and is stated rather than buried**: `list({})` at the root now enumerates the ONE register, so it carries the tab record's leaves (`["tabs","landing","active"]`, `…`) where the second instance's empty register served none — measured `7` paths against `1`, and the register it reports is the profile's own.
- **IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46): D-78's inventory counted six store-like things and this row leaves **five**, the two intended instances being the normal and secure crossings. The package-facing rows among them stay the package's — the package is read and never modified, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape (`D-5`, `D-52`).

## D-80 — The `data.*` tools act on the CENTRAL STORE and on nothing else: `DataSurfaceOptions.store` is REQUIRED, the fallback construction is DELETED, and the cold tier is a name REMOVED under a live process

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** the user's ruling, VERBATIM: **"The data tools should always have been only pointed at the central store, that's what they were added for"** — read beside D-79, which collapsed the surplus instance onto the boot's handle and left `DataSurfaceOptions.store` OPTIONAL because four gating rows were believed to pin a cold tier that a constructed store cannot express.

**Decision.** **The five `provident.data.*` tools are a PRODUCT FEATURE and they survive — acting on the CENTRAL STORE and on nothing else.** `DataSurfaceOptions.store` is **REQUIRED**, and the fallback construction is **DELETED** from `src/main/data/store.ts` — not left unreachable, deleted: `constructStore`, its `journalLengthOf` helper and the seam's runtime import are gone, `ProfileStore` is named through `import type` and `ReturnType<typeof …>` alone, and `StorePort.construct()` — whose `{kind:'ready'}|{kind:'unreachable'}` union carried a refusal arm no surviving code path could produce — is **replaced** by `StorePort.store()`, which returns the caller's instance as handed in. **A surface that can build an instance is a surface one caller away from a second store**, so no arm of the module constructs anything and `src/main/data/` can open no `provident-profile.json`.

- **THE OPTIONS, EACH DECIDED RATHER THAN LEFT BEHIND.** `DataSurfaceOptions` holds three members: `profileDir: string` (**kept**), `journalLength?: number` (**kept**) and `store: ProfileStore` (**required**). `profileDir` is kept because **the reading still reads it and nothing else can**: the reading answers at a NAME — `occupied` / `absent` / `unreachable` — ahead of the instance, a store carries no address for its own file, and without the directory the surface loses §3's `store-unreachable` arm, the cold tier, and the `profile` that every refusal sentence names. It fed the deleted construction as a **second use and never as its reason**. `journalLength` is kept because it always bounded, and still bounds, THIS module's own in-memory change window (`createJournal`, `src/main/data/journal.ts`); the deleted construction sized the CALLER's store from it a second time, and a store's journal is fixed at its own creation, so a supplied instance fixes that figure and this option cannot.
- **THE COLD TIER MOVED, AND THE GATING ROWS WERE RE-DERIVED RATHER THAN DELETED OR WEAKENED** — the twelve rows of `tests/e2e/central-data/scenarios.test.ts`, unmarked and GATING by D-30. A supplied store creates its `filePath` at its own construction (measured 2026-11-02: a zero-length file, `read([])` → `{}`), so the reading of a freshly booted profile answers `occupied` over an EMPTY register. **THE DISTINCTION THAT SETTLED EVERY ROW IS *a tier being cold* VERSUS *a path being absent***: `read` at a name that holds no row answers `{status:'absent'}` whether the register is empty or the file is new, so **all four of the line ranges D-79 cited as pinning an inexpressible cold tier survive their assertion UNCHANGED** — `S-1`'s `list`/`read` (`:134-135` at `48e0157`), `S-7`'s post-delete absence (`:276`), `S-12`'s second-profile read (`:385`) and `S-12`'s foreign-image `list`/`read` (`:405-406`), the last being P-1's boot-cold recovery and unmoved by it. **What genuinely died is the store file's ABSENCE, in three rows and four assertions D-79 did not cite** — S-1's `existsSync`/`readdirSync` (`:136-137`), S-11's (`:373-374`) and S-12's `:388` — each re-derived to the state the product has: the one name stands at **ZERO LENGTH**, no `.tmp`/lock/sidecar stands beside it, and the second profile's own file carries none of the first's row. Each replacement is red if anything ever lands, which is a stronger reading of the sentence than absence was.
- **THE STATE THE PRODUCT NO LONGER HAS, NAMED.** A profile with **no store file at all** is gone: the boot constructs once and that construction creates the name, so "a first-ever profile" is an OPEN register holding nothing, never an absent one. What replaced it as the cold tier is **the name being REMOVED under a live process**: `readProfile` is taken first, so `list`/`read`/`delete` answer cold and `journal` answers its window while the supplied instance still holds the rows it was given, and the instance's next save re-creates the name carrying that register — `behaviour.md` §9's own last row, which is now the cold tier's only home.
- **AND `S-9` WIDENED, BECAUSE THE IMPORT CENSUS WAS BLIND HERE.** It counts importing FILES, so it stayed green while the surface opened a store of its own through the seam — D-79's finding, restated and now gated. It also asserts that **no file under `src/main/data/` binds the seam's construction as a VALUE** (type positions are kept and named, being erased at build and opening nothing), and a `@ts-expect-error` closure asserts that `createDataSurface({ profileDir })` does not COMPILE — so the optionality that made a second store reachable is refused in `typecheck:tests`, not only in prose.
- **THE IMPORT / SEAM COUNT, RE-MEASURED, AND THE SEAM'S OWN COMMENT WAS STALE.** `src/main/storeroom.ts:5` said the app has **TWO** imports of the package; `grep -rn "from 'provident-storeroom'" src/` answers **three files and four statements** at this head, and **no file under `src/main/data/` names the package at all** (measured 2026-11-02). The figure does not move in this pass — the surface already reached the package only through the seam — so the comment was corrected to three, **without the three paths spelled in that file**: two gating rows compute the MCP import closure and red on a cross-module path named inside it (`tests/e2e/security-store-storeroom/isolation.test.ts` `S-11` and `tests/e2e/settings-carrier-retirement/scenarios.test.ts` `S-11`, measured red on the first draft and green after the rewrite).
- **WHAT A REQUIRED INSTANCE OWES, STATED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE DISCOVERED.** Two consequences are recorded here and **fixed nowhere**, because neither is this pass's to rule. (a) Nothing in the type binds `profileDir` to the instance's own `filePath`: the app passes `app.getPath('userData')` and `join(that, PROFILE_FILE_NAME)`, one name, and a caller that points them apart gets the READING's answer — measured deliberately, as the reading's own proof, by `tests/integration/central-data/profile-file.test.ts`'s occupied-name row. (b) Because `src/main/main.ts:132` constructs the store at boot with no guard, a profile name that can never be OCCUPIED is a **boot** fault and not a surface answer: `createProfileStore` throws `FilePathUnreachable` over a directory standing at its `filePath` or under an absent parent (measured 2026-11-02). That hazard arrived with D-79's collapse and is not created here; it is named here because the surface can no longer be the arm that answers it.
- **⟶ THE SECURE CROSSING'S CLAUSE-1 PARAGRAPH IS NOW PART-CLOSED AND PART-STOPPED, BY POINTER AND NEVER BY REWORDING (`D-46`):** `D-84` declares `operator-exclusion-must-stand` at `['operator-exclusion']` — the `mem` address, whose only lawful source under clause 3 a repair at creation is, which is this row's `V5` — so `V1`'s deviation no longer holds at all three of its addresses; **the two `file` addresses are STOPPED and named there**, each being the marker of an import-once arm a creation repair would silently retire (measured: `54` rows over `16` files, seven of them `tests/e2e/`). The clause-1 sentence above and `V1`/`V5` themselves are read through that row.

**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46). D-79's optional-`store` arm and its four-row justification are closed by this row, whose re-derivation found that justification **right about the FILE and wrong about the PATH**. `strata/next-steps.md` row 1 takes a third stage: after D-79's collapse of the instance, the construction itself is now out of `src/main/data/`, and the three legacy store-like things remain to be deleted. The package-facing rows among them stay the package's — the package is read and never modified, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape (D-5, D-52).

**⟶ APPENDED 2026-11-02 — THE BOOT FAULT (b) NAMED IS NOW LEGIBLE AND STILL FATAL: the refusal is caught at its construction site, restated as a sentence, and rethrown.**
`createProfileStore`'s construction is wrapped once, in `src/main/main.ts`, and the catch publishes what failed, the store's own `status` (`FilePathUnreachable`), the path involved and the consequence — `the profile store could not be CONSTRUCTED at boot, so the profile cannot be opened and the app cannot run: status 'FilePathUnreachable' at '<userData>/provident-profile.json'
— the store's own words: "…"`.
**It swallows nothing, substitutes no default, retries nothing and constructs no second instance to work around the first**, and the store's own refusal object rides along as the `Error`'s `cause`, so the evidence is added to rather than replaced.
**THE PREMISE THIS CORRECTS, MEASURED BOTH WAYS ON THIS CHECKOUT (2026-11-02): the refusal was never an UNHANDLED throw.** `main()` is called as `void main().catch(…)`, which already printed it and exited `1`. **What it lacked was a SENTENCE** — it printed the raw object, `{ status: 'FilePathUnreachable', message: "'<path>' is not reachable at construction, …", log: [],
address: null }`, an evidence dump a reader must decode and cannot tell from any other `[provident-main] fatal:`. That is the whole of the difference, and it is why this is a restatement and not a repair.
**FATALITY IS PRESERVED EXACTLY**: same instant, same `exit 1`, measured at ~130 ms from spawn over a directory standing at the profile's name. **AND NO WINDOW EXISTS WHEN IT FIRES** — the construction is `D-19`'s SECOND boot act and `const win = new BrowserWindow` its ELEVENTH — so a stated message reaches a LOG and never a user; **that is what makes the next question a
product call and not a wording one** (the offset is deliberately uncited: the site is the name, D-41).
**WHETHER THE APP SHOULD INSTEAD DEGRADE INTO A STATED, WINDOW-VISIBLE STATE IS OPEN AND NEEDS A RULING.** Nothing here decides it — no default profile, no retry, no second instance, no surface answering in the boot's place. What is declared is that the failure is READABLE; what is left open is whether it should be SURVIVABLE.
**TESTABILITY — DRIVABLE, AND DRIVEN.** The home was an existing one: `tests/e2e/bundle-boot-seam/boot-contract.test.ts` gained **`B-9`**, whose state is a DIRECTORY planted at `<userData>/provident-profile.json` **before the spawn** through a new `prepare` hook on `bootApp` (no later instant exists at which a row could plant it). **It can fail, and it does**: with the catch
reverted the row reports the object dump it found and `the profile store could not be CONSTRUCTED at boot` never matches; restored, `9/9` in that file are green.
**THE ROW IS AN E2E ROW, so it is UNMARKED and GATES** (D-30): `strata-marks.mjs index` moves the readings to **110 gating sites in 20 files, 45 of them retained boundary** sites, and `check` is green.
**AND IT IS A NINTH ROW IN AN EIGHT-ROW DECLARATION**: `docs/L1/bundle-boot-seam.md` §6 declares `B-1`…`B-8`, so the battery's own header now states that `B-9` is the battery's and not §6's — **the §6 declaration is L1's to make, owed and not invented here**.
**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46): (b) stands as written and is now discharged, and the `src/main/main.ts:132` offsets this row and D-79 use still land inside the boot's store region, on the comment that introduces this construction twelve lines above the call.

## D-81 — The module registry's legacy file is DROPPED, not migrated: `src/main/module-store.ts` and its Q-5 arm are DELETED, and an existing `provident-modules.json` is LEFT ON DISK UNREAD

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** the user's ruling, VERBATIM: **"drop it — the same disposition the tabs rows got."** It was given on D-78's second clause, which had measured this registry on this head and HELD it, and it names D-75's own words for what that disposition is: *"There is no migration. No install holds tabs in the operator-settings carrier, so the old rows are dropped rather than migrated. Do not build a boot migration."*

**The authority finding the ruling rests on, all three measured on the held head and re-read here.** **(1)** The registry's file has **no runtime writers**: `src/` held ONE construction and ONE call on it — `createModuleStore({ path: legacyModulesPath })` and its `.list()` — and no site invoked `put`/`remove`/`setDisabled`. **(2)** The `file.modules.<id>` rows on the **secure crossing's** `['operator-app-state']` address ARE authoritative for the product's whole life: a boot over a profile with no legacy file leaves them byte-identical, and every live write is ONE `settingsSurface.commit` of the whole rows list (the module surface's `put`/`remove`/`setDisabled`, read back through `moduleRecordsOf`, `syncModuleRouter`, the server's `moduleStore` option, `IPC_MODULE_GET` and Y-1). **(3)** The arm was **not dead**, which is why D-78 held it: reachable on EVERY boot, guarded only by the file's own existence, its `rmSync` making it run once — and a re-planted file **OUTRANKS a standing row** at that boot (`alpha@1.0.0` → `9.9.9-JSON-WINS`). D-75 is scoped to the TABS rows and names this registry nowhere, so the loss of module state for a profile not booted since `3673013` was unruled — and is now ruled.

**WHAT IS DELETED, WITH LINE COUNTS — 422 lines of module plus the arm, and nothing else under `src/`.** `src/main/module-store.ts` (**207** lines, the raw-`fs` registry: `ModuleRecord`/`ModuleStoreStatus`/`ModuleStore`/`ModuleStoreOptions`, `sanitizeRecord`, `load` and `createModuleStore`'s six verbs and atomic `persist`) and `tests/module-store.test.ts` (**215** lines, **12** spec sites, on notice) are **DELETED**. The three INTERFACES survive and MOVED to `src/main/mcp-server.ts`, which is the module tools' own surface (`handleModuleTool`, `syncModuleRouter`, `ProvidentMcpServerOptions.moduleStore` are their three consumers); they are types only — no `node:fs`, no construction, no file, and deliberately no file NAME, since the MCP closure's byte census allows exactly one foreign `provident-*.json` literal. **From `src/main/main.ts`, five things go**: the `legacyModulesPath` local, `createModuleStore({ path: … })`, its `.list()`, the projection loop with its `rowsWith` call and the conditional `settingsSurface.commit(composed)`, and `rmSync(legacyModulesPath, { force: true })` — with the `node:fs` import itself, so **this entry now imports nothing from `node:fs` at all**. **`rowsWith` STAYS, and this was verified rather than assumed**: it is not arm-only — `modulePayloadWith` (`main.ts`) is its live caller, `module-tools.test.ts`/`module-e2e.test.ts` drive the members that thread it, and `tests/unit/settings-carrier-retirement/main-source.test.ts` `MS-4` pins its declaration, its caller and the duplicate-`name` refusal that makes the rule mandatory. **No arm-only helper survived it.**

**THE FILE STAYS ON DISK, AND THAT IS THE DISPOSITION, NOT AN OVERSIGHT.** `rmSync` is what removed `<userData>/provident-modules.json`; with the arm gone **nothing opens, parses, rewrites or unlinks that name** — it is not READ (its records reach no row) and not REMOVED (a profile that carried one keeps it byte-for-byte), which is D-75's own *"stop projecting, not delete"* applied to this registry. The composition states that where the arm stood, because an unexplained absence reads as a defect. **AND THE BOOT MUST NOT FAIL ON SUCH A FILE — MEASURED, 2026-11-02, on the BUILT bundle**: a scratch profile seeded with two records whose `hash` is the correct sha256 of their `source` (clean under the deleted `sanitizeRecord`, so under the old arm they would have LOADED and the file would have been REMOVED) booted to a live stdio server (`started=true`, `9` tools listed), answered `module.list` → **`[]`**, left `provident-modules.json` standing at the **same sha256 and the same 415 bytes**, left no `file.modules.*` row and neither record's name in the operator record, and was killed and reaped to **0 survivors**.

**THE CENSUS CONSEQUENCE.** `tests/store-security.test.ts`'s userData-literal row is **re-derived, never deleted**, from `toEqual(['provident-modules.json'])` to **`toEqual([])`** — it is the witness that this name is no longer written, and it now also witnesses that the app no longer looks for it; the row beside it pins the comment that says so. `S-10`/`S-11`/`ISO-6` do not move: no `tests/e2e/` file named the store, its file or its call (**0** hits, re-measured) and the MCP closure's allowed literal set is unmoved, because the name was never in it. `store-compliance-live.mjs` needs **no entry in either list** — not as WRITTEN (nothing writes it) and not as SEEDED-RETIRED (nothing reads it as a seed) — and a `.json` outside the declared set was already `SC-B-01`'s finding. `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`'s operator-profile witness census **sheds** this name and **gains** `provident-profile.json`, the normal crossing's own file, which it never named: a STRENGTHENING, so both crossings are observed. The suites: `npm test` moves `3,095 → 3,083` passed of `3,097 → 3,085` over `124 → 123` files (the deleted file's 12 sites, read by running it), `srcTypeScriptFiles 50 → 49`, and the marks `112 → 111` files with `2,758 → 2,746` on-notice sites, REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` and never hand-edited; gating stays `110` sites in `20` files.
**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46): D-78's second clause stands as the measurement that asked for this ruling and its OWED line is discharged here; D-75, D-79 and D-80 are read and left standing. `strata/next-steps.md` row 1's stage *(iv)* is closed by it and the purge's only remaining deletion is the app's frozen graph store (`src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts`); `strata/pending.md` row 5 leaves the file with its reason recorded there. The package-facing rows stay the package's — the package is read and never modified, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape (D-5, D-52).

## D-82 — THE DESIGN CONVENTION FOR THE APP'S DATA FLOW, audited clause by clause: the tab record CONFORMS on all four and is the only family that does, the secure crossing DEVIATES on clause 1 at all three of its addresses, and the app-state rows are the weakest family

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, VERBATIM — *"Confirm that the migrated verbs follow the design convention: / Constraints enforce the existence of required data, repairing with placeholders (ex. landing page for tabs) where data is missing / Listeners carry data back to renderer or other consumers on change / Boot data comes from file or constraint repairs / Client-driven writes are driven by external events (ex. MCP, UI, APIs)"* — plus the measurements below, all driven 2026-11-02 on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.0` and the real modules.

**Decision.** **That paragraph is the DESIGN CONVENTION FOR THE APP'S DATA FLOW**, read as four clauses, and every durable family is measured against all four. **THE FAMILIES, ENUMERATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED — SIX OVER TWO CROSSINGS AND NO THIRD**: the tab record at `['tabs']` and the `data.*` surface's arbitrary paths on the **normal** crossing (`createProfileStore`, `src/main/main.ts:156`); the operator settings record at `['operator-settings']`, the operator exclusion at `['operator-exclusion']` and the app-state rows at `['operator-app-state']` — which carry the module registry's `file.modules.<id>` leaves — on the **secure** crossing (`storeCreate`, `src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:405`); and the retired carriers `provident-security.json`/`provident-settings.json`, read once and written by nothing. **The audit's expected family (iii) is not a thing of its own**: the secure crossing IS the operator-settings instance, so there is no third store and no separate security store to audit.

**CLAUSE 1 — CONSTRAINTS ENFORCE EXISTENCE AND REPAIR WITH PLACEHOLDERS.** **CONFORMS at the tab record, and only there.** Five declarations stand at `['tabs']` (`tabsConstraintDeclarations`, `src/main/tabs-record/index.ts:937-943`) and the boot batches all five (`src/main/main.ts:158`); `tabs-must-stand` refuses an absent value and its repair MINTS the landing row, and the three field pairs repair to `'untitled'`, `'tabs-landing-page'` and a fresh stamp. Measured: a scratch profile **0 bytes long** answers `read(['tabs'])` with the landing entry seated and active before any act, and a commit carrying `label: ''` lands `label: 'untitled'` with `repaired: true`. **DEVIATES at the secure crossing, at ALL THREE addresses, and the prior reading is CONFIRMED rather than refuted**: its construction is `storeCreate({ journalLength: cap(options.journalLength), listeners: [], filePath: join(options.userDataDir, FILE_NAME) })` — **zero constraints and zero listeners, and no `constraints` key at all**. Nothing guards an absent record, an absent exclusion or an absent row list: measured, a fresh profile answers `read()` `{token:null, enabled:['read','dispatch'], exclusion:'mcp-enabled'}` and `state()` `{rows:[], legacy:'absent'}` **while all three addresses answer `AddressNotFound`**, and a record seeded as `{}` is **STILL `{}` after the boot** — the placeholder is substituted at the READ (`FIRST_RUN`, `SAFE_PAIR`) and never repaired into the record. A missing `file.modules.<id>` row is simply absent (`list()` `[]`, `status().loaded` `[]`); the hash re-derivation is a consumer-side quarantine, not an existence guard. **DEVIATES at `data.*`**: it declares no pair of its own and only inherits the tab record's five — measured, `write({path:['tabs'], value:42})` answers `committed` and the landing record stands (the pairs repaired it), a stray `['tabs','rows']` member STANDS and `data.read` serves it, and a write whose parent is absent is refused `parent-absent` rather than repaired.

**CLAUSE 2 — LISTENERS CARRY CHANGE BACK TO A CONSUMER.** **CONFORMS at the tab record**: `standing.listen(handle, [TABS_RECORD_ADDRESS])` then `standing.register(handle, body)` (`src/main/tabs-record/index.ts:1108-1109`), the body contains the caller's throw and writes nothing, and the chain is `tabs-surface`'s `record.subscribe(() => options.onChanged?.())` (`src/main/tabs-surface/index.ts:75`) → the boot's `onChanged` broadcasting `IPC_TABS_CHANGED` to every window (`src/main/main.ts:178-182`) → the preload's `onChanged` → the renderer's `tabs-client`, which re-reads. Measured: one delivery per committed `commit`, `remove` and root-`delete`, one for a descendant write, **ZERO** for a `mem` write at `['elsewhere']`. **The delivery carries a SIGNAL, not the data** — the address and nothing else, and the consumer re-reads, which is the convention's "carry data back" implemented as one notification per change. **CONFORMS at the operator record and the exclusion**: `subscribe` binds `[RECORD_ADDRESS, EXCLUSION_ADDRESS]` (`src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:550`) with `listen`+`register` per address, and measured 2 deliveries for two record writes and 1 for a transition, the consumer being `settings-surface`'s `onDelivery` re-patching the gate and the tier-4 holder. **DEVIATES at the app-state rows**: `['operator-app-state']` is not in that list, and measured `writeAppState` delivered **0** — a module row's change reaches the renderer and the MCP tools **only by their own request** (`module.list`, `IPC_MODULE_GET`), never by push. **DEVIATES at `data.*`**: no listener anywhere; its `journal` is an in-process window the surface appends to itself and no `data.*` change crosses a process boundary.

**CLAUSE 3 — BOOT DATA COMES FROM A FILE OR A CONSTRAINT REPAIR.** **CONFORMS at the tab record** (the registration-instant repair mints the root), **at the operator record** and **at the app-state rows** — measured: a written record's token, enabled list, cap and a `file.modules.alpha` row all come back from `provident-operator-settings.json` at the next boot. **DEVIATES at the exclusion**, a `mem` address: measured, a boot that transitioned it to `'mcp-disabled'` boots again to `'mcp-enabled'`, its value coming from neither a file nor a repair but from the code constant `SAFE_PAIR`. **The Q-5 arm's deletion is confirmed and its consequence measured**: boot data for the module rows now comes only from the rows standing in the operator file, and a profile carrying a 265-byte `provident-modules.json` with a **correct** sha256 boots to `list()` `[]`, no `file.modules.*` row, `status()` `{corrupt:false, quarantined:[], loaded:[]}`, and the same 265 bytes on disk afterwards. **Nothing renderer-side is in this clause's scope but one thing is worth naming**: `hydrate` and `evaluateTabsBootStep` construct an IN-REALM node graph and one `file.tabs.order` commit from the hand-off's rows — a re-projection, and the frozen graph store is legacy under the purge, so the clause's demand on it is that it stop existing rather than that it be repaired.

**CLAUSE 4 — CLIENT-DRIVEN WRITES DRIVE OFF EXTERNAL EVENTS.** **CONFORMS at every family**, with one boot-instantiated projection flagged rather than hidden. Tab record: `IPC_TABS_COMMIT`/`IPC_TABS_REMOVE` (a renderer act), `provident.data.*` calls (MCP), the five pairs' repairs, and the existence repair at boot. Operator record and exclusion: `IPC_SECURITY_SET` and `IPC_SECURITY_EXCLUSION` only — the boot's `attach` writes no store, its three calls being a tier-4 holder write and two SINK patches on the gate. App-state rows: `module.install`/`module.update` (MCP) through `put`, `IPC_MODULE_SET_DISABLED` (an operator act) through `setDisabled`, and Y-2's `STORE_FILE_PUT` committing the renderer's `file.*` projection — whose `remove` verb has **no `src/` caller at all**. `data.*`: the five `provident.data.*` tool calls and nothing else, since the five `data.*` bridge members still have no answer site. **THE ONE WRITE WITH NO EXTERNAL CAUSE IS Y-2'S AT BOOT**: `evaluateTabsBootStep` performs one `file.tabs.order` commit when the hand-off carries a non-empty sequence, and the renderer's frozen store pushes every `file`-tier commit through `crossing.put` into `STORE_FILE_PUT` — a re-projection of file data back into the file, inside the allowed BOOT class, flagged here because it is the nearest thing this app has to a write nothing external asked for. **⟶ AND THIS FLAG IS CLOSED, BY POINTER AND NOT BY REWORDING (`D-46`):** the write named in this sentence is **DELETED** — `D-83` removes `evaluateTabsBootStep`, its declared outcome and its one call, keeps the three arms and the `H-4` guard as that step's measured record, and puts the `order` seat under a sixth declaration tested at the store's creation; the clause-3 consequence is that same row's. **No timer, poll, interval or on-read re-derivation writes any family**: the only `setTimeout`s under `src/main/` are the MCP readiness timeouts.

**THE HEADLINE.** **The tab record is the one family that conforms on all four clauses**; the secure crossing **conforms on clause 2 for two of its three addresses and deviates on clause 1 at all three and on clause 3 for the exclusion**; **the app-state rows are the weakest family** — no constraint and no listener — while their boot data is the file and nothing else; and `data.*` **conforms on 3 and 4 and deviates on 1 and 2**. **The smallest change that closes each deviation, NAMED AND NOT BUILT**: a declaration at each of the secure crossing's three addresses whose body refuses an absent or blank value and whose repair writes `FIRST_RUN` / `SAFE_PAIR` / `{rows: []}` — **a NEW CONSTRAINT, and `D-78` permits no layer to judge its own design**; `APP_STATE_ADDRESS` added to the two a subscription already binds — **a NEW ADDRESS in a listener set whose consumer does not exist**; and the surface answering its receipt with the repairs that fired — **`D-79`'s proposed guard, which moves L2 §2/§3's closed vocabulary and needs a ruling**. The exclusion's clause-3 deviation closes with the first of those and with nothing smaller.

**⟶ THE SECURE CROSSING'S CLAUSE-1 PARAGRAPH IS NOW PART-CLOSED AND PART-STOPPED, BY POINTER AND NEVER BY REWORDING (`D-46`):** `D-84` declares `operator-exclusion-must-stand` at `['operator-exclusion']` — the `mem` address, whose only lawful source under clause 3 a repair at creation is, which is this row's `V5` — so `V1`'s deviation no longer holds at all three of its addresses; **the two `file` addresses are STOPPED and named there**, each being the marker of an import-once arm a creation repair would silently retire (measured: `54` rows over `16` files, seven of them `tests/e2e/`). The clause-1 sentence above and `V1`/`V5` themselves are read through that row.

**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46). Its register is D-71 to D-81: the change push returned to the record root, a repair's credit made explicit, a repair write made to name its commit, the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero, the landing seat derived and its entry id moved, the renderer client's Stage A, the two-crossing ruling with its purge, the surplus instance collapsed, the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store, and the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape.

**⟶ ITS `V3` CELL IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER, AND NOT ONE WORD OF THIS ROW IS REWORDED** (`D-46`). The smallest change this row named — *"a NEW ADDRESS in a listener set whose consumer does not exist"*, `APP_STATE_ADDRESS` added to the two a subscription already binds — is **BUILT AT `D-104`**, where the consumer turns out to EXIST: the app's one reading of the app-state rows is `SecurePanels`' `module-status`/`module-list` pair, the address is in the instance's one subscription, and the request-time read that stood beside it is DELETED rather than joined. `V3` is therefore CLOSED there, and clause 2's *"DEVIATES at the app-state rows"* is what this row measured at its own date, read through that one.

**⟶ AND ITS `V2` CELL IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AS A MISATTRIBUTION, NEVER REWORDED** (`D-46`, `D-105`). `V2` recorded `data.*` as deviating at clause 1, and **THAT READING WAS WRONG**: `data.*` CONFORMS BY CONSTRUCTION — it IS the package surface, and the package executes the constraints on every write — so the deviation was never in the surface. What replaced it is the obligation on every family's DECLARATION to enforce its own invariant against ANY caller, and the families that failed are named at `D-105`: `panes-must-stand`, `focus-must-stand`, `zones-must-stand`, `operator-record-must-stand`, `operator-exclusion-must-stand` and `operator-app-state-must-stand`. The clause-1 paragraph above stands as this row wrote it, read through that one.

## D-83 — THE IMPERATIVE BOOT WRITE IS DELETED AND ITS SUBJECT BECOMES A SIXTH DECLARATION TESTED AT THE STORE'S CREATION: `order-names-its-tabs`, whose repair writes the corrected `order` as ONE legal record

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, VERBATIM — *"evaluateTabsBootStep needs to be a repair following a constraint tested on store creation"* — plus the measurements below, all driven 2026-11-02 on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.0`.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN, AND IT DECIDES THE PASS.** *What the step did*: `evaluateTabsBootStep(store, handedOrder)` (`src/renderer/renderer.ts:763-786`) had three arms — an ARGUMENT-DOMAIN arm answering `{written:false, receipt:null, refusal:'non-sequence-argument'}` for anything that was not a `readonly string[]` of non-empty ids or `undefined` and touching no store; the `H-4` GUARD answering the same no-write value with `refusal:null` for an ABSENT or EMPTY sequence, because committing `[]` on a cold tier MINTED the membership leaf and the reserved landing entry through the repair that evaluates it, emitting repair events on a tier that held nothing; and ONE EVALUATED WRITE, `store.commit('file.tabs.order', [...carried])`, answering `{written:true, receipt, refusal:null}`. Its outcome carried four members — `written`, `receipt`, and `refusal`'s two tokens — and the store's receipt was the only place a repair it fired was observable. **The seat it wrote is `['tabs','order']`** — a stored array `viewOf` reads out of the ONE whole value at `['tabs']`, `composeRecord` writes inside that value, and `remove` rewrites without the removed id — so the step's write was a re-projection of file data back into the file with no external event behind it (D-82's clause-4 flag).

**AND THE STEP HAS NO COUNTERPART AT THE CROSSING, WHICH IS WHY IT DIES RATHER THAN MOVES.** Its sequence came from `bootHandoff` — the OPERATOR-SETTINGS carrier's rows, a RENDERER-side hand-off handed to the renderer's own frozen graph store — while the record the app keeps is read from `provident-profile.json` by the boot's one central store (`src/main/main.ts:156`). **Nothing at that construction can know a "handed order" at all**, so there is no repair to move and nothing to declare there; the only order the boot must repair is the one the FILE carries.

**WHAT ALREADY REPAIRED A BAD SEAT, AND WHAT DID NOT — the question that decides whether the sixth pair is redundant.** Driven on the batch of five, one fresh instance per arm, over a profile written by a store carrying NO pair and then constructed over: the FOUR wrapped bodies (`exactly-one-active` and the three field pairs) read through the boot's holder, which is bound only AFTER `createProfileStore` answers, so **they ABSTAIN at the creation instant** — `read()` immediately after the construction answered `order: []` with `rows: []` over a record holding `tab-1` and no `order`, the same over `order: 'x'` and over `order: []`, and the same over `order: ['ghost']`. Those FOUR are repaired — but only at the FIRST LATER WRITE, measured: the same profile's first `commit` answered `{status:'committed', repaired:true}` and left a legal record. **FOUR MORE ARE REPAIRED BY NOTHING**: `order: ['landing','tab-1']` with `tab-1` ABSENT, because `exactly-one-active` counts SURVIVORS — ids the post state both seats and holds — so one held row under a two-name seat is ONE active and the gate PASSES, and `read()` named a tab the record did not hold until an unrelated field repair happened to drop it; `order: ['landing','landing']`, where the same row is counted twice and the record became UNRECOVERABLE — a later `commit` was REFUSED `ConstraintPassRefused`, *"the pair tabs#exactly-one-active violated a re-run and its repair had already been applied"*, because both seats named the one held row; the LEGACY STRING SEAT `{order:['landing'], landing:'landing'}`, which `LANDING_ENTRY_KEY`'s own docblock hands to "the first whole-record write"; and `order: ['landing', 7]`, a non-string member `viewFrom` carries past. **So the pair is NOT redundant: four states are unlawful at the creation instant and four are unlawful anywhere, and no existing pair judges the seat they all read the family from.**

**Decision.** A **sixth declaration** rides the batch — id `order-names-its-tabs`, listed second, beside `tabs-must-stand` at `['tabs']` — **UNWRAPPED by the `body` helper** for the existence pair's reason: a registration's context carries `value: readCurrent(store, address)`, so the body judges the RECORD and needs no handle, and the wrapped bodies' null-store abstention cannot silence it at the instant it is the only one that can act. It REFUSES a seat that is absent, is not an array, is empty, carries a non-string or empty-string member, repeats an id, or names an id the record holds no ROW under — in ONE sentence naming the seat and not the act, *"the tab record's 'order' seat carries no sequence of its own tabs, once each"* — and its repair writes the corrected order through `healedFamily`, the module's ONE home of the convergence rule, as ONE whole legal record through `writeWholeRecord`, the module's one repair-write path at either instant (three positions at a registration, `writeJoined`'s fourth at a landing), answering the credited arm through `repairAnswer` exactly as D-72/D-73 require. **`healedFamily` gained the dedupe the duplicate arm needs** and `mintRecord` now routes through `writeWholeRecord`, so no second answer to "what is a corrected record" exists. **AND THE PAIR STANDS AT `['tabs']` AND NOT AT `['tabs','order']`, WHICH IS A MEASUREMENT**: a probe pair at the seat's own address is handed the SEAT at a registration instant (`['landing','ghost']`, `tier: null`) and the ACT's whole value at a write (`tier: 'file'`), so its repair could write an `order` and never the rows that order names — and `{order: ['landing']}` over a record holding no `landing` row is the state the pair exists to refuse. The record is what a legal repair must leave, so the pair stands where `composeRecord` writes the order and `viewOf` reads it.

**THE IMPERATIVE WRITE IS DELETED, NOT REDUCED.** `evaluateTabsBootStep`, its `TabsBootOutcome` and its one call in `renderer.ts`'s `main()` are GONE; a headstone stands where the exports were and at the call site, and `TABS_ORDER_NAME`, `readTabsRef`, `mintTabId`, `closeTab`, `driveTabsLandingPage` and the Y-1/Y-2/Y-3 hand-off are byte-unmoved. **The register's drive is deleted with it** (`shippedBootSeamDrive`, `BootSeamReading`, and the seam table's fourth entry), and the battery's `B-8`/`G-5` become the deletion row and the batch row — `B-8` reading the WIRING'S CODE WITH COMMENT LINES AND BLOCK COMMENTS ELIDED, with its own positive control (the raw bytes DO carry the headstone's spelling), because a naive byte read passes on a comment and a count over the raw bytes read 3 where the code holds 0.

**MEASURED AFTER IT, THE SAME EIGHT ARMS AT THE SAME INSTANT**: every one of them now stands legal before any act — the four unsalvageable seats take the landing floor (a fresh row, or the FILE's own row where one stands, its label and stamp preserved: `{order:['landing'], landing:{… 'label':'kept-from-the-file'}}`), the dangling and repeated seats are dropped to `['landing']`, and the legacy string is replaced by the row. **And the pair does not double-repair**: a legal two-tab record stands BYTE-IDENTICAL through the construction and a later clean `commit` over it answers `{status:'committed'}` with NO `repaired` member — measured, both halves. The cross-kind pattern is unmoved at six pairs plus a live listener at one address, and the four legs are **0/0/0/0** with `npm test` at `3,093 passed / 2 skipped` of `3,095` over `123` files (`3,083` of `3,085` before this pass; the ten rows gained are `tests/integration/store-tabs-record/crossing.test.ts`'s), one of which reds the moment the sixth declaration leaves the batch (measured: 10 rows red, 21 green, on that revert, and green restored).

**IT CLOSES D-82's CLAUSE 3 FOR THE `order` SEAT AND ITS CLAUSE-4 FLAG WITH IT**: boot data for the tab record now comes from the file OR from a constraint's repair at the creation instant, and the one write this app had with no external cause behind it no longer exists. **`D-82`'s deviation list is updated BY POINTER AND NEVER REWORDED** (D-46). Its register is D-71 to D-82: the change push returned to the record root, a repair's credit made explicit, a repair write made to name its commit, the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero, the landing entry's id decided, the landing seat derived and that id moved, the renderer client's Stage A, the two-crossing ruling with its purge, the surplus instance collapsed, the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store, the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated, and the data-flow convention audited clause by clause. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape.
