## D-84 — THE `mem` PAIR'S EXISTENCE BECOMES THE INSTANCE'S ONE DECLARATION, TESTED AT THE STORE'S CREATION; THE TWO `file` ADDRESSES ARE **STOPPED AND NAMED**, because each one is an import-once arm's MARKER

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-02 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"V1 and V5 close together: V5's smallest closing change is V1's first declaration"*; *"Add a declaration at each of the three addresses, in `tabsMustStand`'s shape"*; and *"If a declaration cannot be added without reddening a gating row, stop that one, keep the others, and report — a partial landing with the obstruction named beats a red tree"*; plus the measurements below, all driven 2026-11-02 on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.0`.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN, AND IT DECIDES THE PASS.** The secure crossing's construction was `storeCreate({journalLength, listeners: [], filePath})` with **no `constraints` key and no listener**, so `D-82`'s `V1` holds at none of its three addresses and `V5` holds at `['operator-exclusion']`. One fresh instance per arm: **all three addresses answer `AddressNotFound`** on a fresh profile and over a register seeded `{}`, and `read()` answers `{token:null, enabled:['read','dispatch'], exclusion:'mcp-enabled', tier4:false}` while `readAppState()` answers `{rows:[], legacy:'absent', …}` — **the placeholder is substituted at the READ and nothing is repaired into the record**, and a record seeded as `{}` is still `{}` after the boot. The three substitutions are `FIRST_RUN` (`??` in `read`), `fromStored`'s per-member arms, and `SAFE_PAIR.exclusion`; **`legacy` and both import-once arms are marked by the same absence** (`recordedAt` and the record-address probe). **And the registration instant hands a body `{address, attempt:null, op:null, tier:null, value}`** — measured per address: `value` is the object the address holds, `{}` where it holds an empty one and `undefined` where it holds nothing, and the BODY's record carries no `write` member, so the `tabsMustStand` shape transfers unaltered. The file is created exclusively at **0 bytes** and stays there through every registration repair: **a creation repair lands in the instance's memory and never in the file.**

**THE DECLARATION THAT LANDS, AND THE TWO THAT ARE STOPPED WITH THEIR OBSTRUCTION MEASURED.** `OPERATOR_CONSTRAINT_DECLARATIONS` (`src/main/operator-settings/index.ts`) rides the construction's `constraints` key with **one** pair — `operator-exclusion-must-stand` at `['operator-exclusion']`, id exported nowhere because the instance is its own only consumer — whose body judges `context.value`, refuses an address holding nothing in a sentence naming the pair and not the act, and whose repair writes `SAFE_PAIR`'s own `{exclusion:'mcp-enabled'}` as ONE whole legal value at `mem`, through the registration's three-position `write` member, answering `{ok:true, op}` through `repairAnswer` (`D-72`/`D-73`; a landing-instant arm writes nothing and answers the failed arm, because no route of this instance can leave the pair absent and this module supplies `write`'s fourth position **nowhere** — `signatures.md` §2). **`V5` therefore closes and `V1` closes at one of its three addresses**, and the two `file` addresses do not, for a reason no layer may decide alone: **each is the MARKER of an import-once arm, so a creation repair there makes its marker permanently true and retires an import silently.** Measured, one fresh instance per arm: with a declaration at `['operator-settings']`, a profile seeded with a retired `provident-security.json` carrying a token, three grants, a cap and one entry answers `token:null` / `enabled:['read','dispatch']` where this head answers all four — **the retired operator's record is LOST**; with one at `['operator-app-state']`, `legacy` reads `'recorded'` on **every** arm (`absent`, `imported`, `corrupt` alike) and the retired carrier's rows never import. Driven through the real suites, the two `file` declarations red **54 rows over 16 files** — among them seven `tests/e2e/` rows (`S-7` of `security-store-storeroom`; `S-2`, `S-3`, `S-4`, `S-5`, `S-6` and `S-8` of `settings-carrier-retirement`) — so they are **reported, not built**. Closing them needs a marker a repair cannot forge (the file's own absence, read by something other than the store), which is a vocabulary ruling and not an audit's to invent. **⟶ AND THAT STOPPED HALF IS CLOSED, BY POINTER AND NEVER BY REWORDING (`D-46`):** `D-85` declares both `file` addresses and each of their repairs PERFORMS what the address's absence keyed — the `54`-red figure above is the reading of its own date, and the marker's truth is now the repair's own act rather than the retirement of an import.

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT COST.** The boot's file is **0 bytes before and after** (asserted in the row below and unmoved across four arms: fresh, `{}`, a retired security seed, a retired carrier seed), its first write is byte-identical at `65`/`116` bytes, its second at `138`/`189`, both retired imports still run, and `legacy`'s four words are unchanged — **the one observable difference through the declared surface is the journal's first caller-visible ordinal, `cursor: 1` becoming `cursor: 2`**, because the construction's repair lands the first op. **The read-side defaults are NOT deleted and are NOT redundant**: `SAFE_PAIR.exclusion` still answers the non-token member arm and the refused-read arm (measured, a `{exclusion:7}` written at that address passes the body and the read answers the safe pair), and `FIRST_RUN` is still the absent-record arm and the per-member default inside `fromStored`/`importOnce` for every record that does stand — deleting either is a separate and riskier act. **The convergence rule is satisfied by construction and needs no composition**: the three addresses are disjoint top-level keys, a pass is opened at one address and a repair writes at that address only, so **two of these declarations cannot refuse in one pass** — measured, all three stood at once after a construction carrying all three, each holding exactly its own placeholder.

**THE ROWS, EACH RED ON REVERT**: `tests/unit/security-store-storeroom/creation-declarations.test.ts` reads the configuration the module ACTUALLY hands `storeCreate` (captured at the package boundary, never read off `src/**` — so no row here can pass on a comment naming an address) and constructs a REAL store with that exact array: the creation instant against an `AddressNotFound` control, the agreement of the repair with the read, the pair's **re-mint at a second boot**, the existence-only arm, the landed op, and the batch's membership as a census. All six red with the declaration removed (`expected [] to deeply equal [['operator-exclusion', …]]`; `expected {cursor: 1} to deeply equal {cursor: 2}`) and all six green restored. **`npm test` is `3,099 passed / 2 skipped` of `3,101` over `124` files** — `6` more than `D-83`'s `3,093` of `3,095` over `123` — and the four legs are **0/0/0/0**.

**WHAT REMAINS, NAMED AND NOT BUILT**: `V1` at `['operator-settings']` and `['operator-app-state']`, behind the marker ruling above; `V2` (clause 1 at `data.*`, needing a vocabulary ruling); `V3` (`APP_STATE_ADDRESS` in a listener set whose consumer does not exist); `V4` (the surface answering its receipt with the repairs that fired, which moves L2 §2/§3's closed vocabulary and needs a ruling). Its register is D-71 to D-83: the change push returned to the record root, a repair's credit made explicit, a repair write made to name its commit, the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero, the landing entry's id decided, the landing seat derived and that id moved, the renderer client's Stage A, the two-crossing ruling with its purge, the surplus instance collapsed, the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store, the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated, the data-flow convention audited clause by clause, and the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape.

**⟶ AND `V3`, THE ONE THIS SENTENCE LEFT OPEN, IS TAKEN AT `D-104` BY POINTER** (`D-46`): the address is bound in the instance's one subscription, its delivery reaches the realm over `IPC_MODULE_CHANGED`, and the shape it fills drives the pane that displays the module rows — so *"a listener set whose consumer does not exist"* is a measurement of THIS row's date and not a standing state. `V1`'s two `file` addresses remain as filed.

## D-85 — THE ABSENCE-KEYED IMPORT GOES *IN* THE REPAIR: the two `file` addresses carry declarations whose repairs PERFORM what their absence used to key, the reads stop importing, and `D-84`'s 54-red stop closes at ZERO rows

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"If their absence keys another action, then that is part of the repair shape"*; plus the measurements below, all driven 2026-11-03 on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.0`.

**THE GROUND, ESTABLISHED BEFORE ANY ARM WAS CHANGED, AND IT DECIDES THE PASS — the ARMS, with the expression that answers each.** `importOnce(dir)` reads `<dir>/provident-security.json` by ONE `readFileSync` and answers a `Row` on its object arm, `undefined` on FOUR (`ENOENT`, unparsable, a non-plain parse, an unreadable/directory path) — it has no `'corrupt'` word — and was called at `index.ts`'s construction **iff** `store.read(['operator-settings'],'file')` threw; the result was held as `overlay` and served by `read()` as `plain(durable) ? fromStored(durable) : (overlay ?? FIRST_RUN)`. `importAppStateOnce(dir)` reads `<dir>/provident-settings.json` by ONE `readFileSync` and answers `NO_IMPORT` (`legacy:'absent'`) on `ENOENT`, `{legacy:'corrupt'}` on `EACCES`/`EISDIR`/unparsable/non-plain/a foreign top-level key/a `schemaVersion` other than `'1'`, and `{rows, legacy:'imported', unrepresentable}` otherwise, splitting the rows `representable` admits from the names it does not; it was called **iff** `!recordedAt(read(['operator-app-state'],'file'))`, and `legacy` was **that call's own word** — `'recorded'` iff a record stood, else the arm, fixed at construction and served for the process's life. `FIRST_RUN` is `{token:null, enabled:['read','dispatch']}`; `toStored`/`fromStored` are inverses over the four durable members (a cleared member written as ABSENCE, a member failing its rule falling to its first-run value); `fromAppState` is `recordedAt` (a plain object carrying a genuine `rows` array) plus a per-row skip rule; `appLegacy` was derived from the import outcome at the read. **THE ACCEPTANCE BAR, ENUMERATED AS ONE ANSWER PER ARM — a fresh profile; a retired `provident-security.json` with a token, three grants, a cap and an entry; a retired settings carrier with rows; a carrier with an `unrepresentable` row; a corrupt either; a directory at either path; and a profile already `'recorded'` — every one driven before and after, and every answer of the `read()`/`readAppState()`/`legacy` set identical to the character.**

**THE DECLARATIONS — THREE, IN `tabsMustStand`'s AND `D-84`'s SHAPE, IN ONE BATCH MINTED PER CONSTRUCTION.** `declarationsFor(userDataDir)` replaces the module-level list, because a `file` repair must know where the retired carriers stand and `cfg.constraints`' act is taken while the caller holds no handle (`guide §7.2` (1)). `operator-record-must-stand` at `['operator-settings']` refuses everything `read()` cannot read (`plain(context.value)`) and its repair **performs `importOnce` and writes `toStored(imported ?? FIRST_RUN)` as ONE whole legal record**; `operator-app-state-must-stand` at `['operator-app-state']` refuses an absent row list (`recordedAt`) and its repair **performs `importAppStateOnce` and writes `{rows, legacy, unrepresentable}` as ONE record carrying BOTH the rows and the word** — because the word was derived from the import outcome, and once the record stands the derivation must come from the record (`legacyOf`: a record's own three words are served, and a record standing WITHOUT one — the `{rows}` a caller's `writeAppState` composes — answers `'recorded'`, which is the pre-`D-85` answer for that arm). `operator-exclusion-must-stand` is byte-unmoved (`D-84`). Each body judges `context.value`, unwrapped by any handle-needing helper, at the registration instant, and each repair writes through the registration's own THREE positions and returns the credited arm through `repairAnswer` (`D-72`, `D-73`).

**THE IMPORT IS AN `fs` READ RUNNING INSIDE A REPAIR AT THE REGISTRATION INSTANT, AND THAT IS LAWFUL — MEASURED, AND MEASURED AT THE RECORD AND NOT AT THE RECEIPT.** Nothing in the package constrains a repair's body beyond the answer it returns; the route is the repair's alone and the body is the caller's code (`guide §7.2` (4)). **THE RESULT REACHES THE WRITTEN RECORD**: a profile seeded with the retired security record, booted, then flushed by a write at the OTHER address (the save a `file`-tier commit takes), leaves `provident-operator-settings.json` holding `{"operator-settings":{"enabled":["graph","read","module"],"entries":{"x-kept":{"a":1}},"maxJournalLength":9,"token":"tok"}}` — the imported values at the address, in the store's own bytes, with no `read()` in the path; and the row-list address holds `{"rows":[{"name":"file.layout.dock","value":"left"}],"legacy":"imported","unrepresentable":["file.tabs.order"]}`. A repair whose write merely succeeded would leave `FIRST_RUN` there instead. **A CORRUPT CARRIER STILL ANSWERS `'corrupt'`, WITH THE RECORD STANDING** — five failure shapes driven, the four-member answer unmoved, the word written into the record rather than fallen through.

**IDEMPOTENCE, MEASURED OVER SUCCESSIVE BOOTS BY OP COUNT AND BY CARRIER BYTES, WITH THE LIMIT STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** After ONE `file`-tier write both records are on the disk, and the next boot repairs **only** the `mem` pair — its first caller write answers `cursor: 2`, one repair, not three — and answers the same rows, the same arm and byte-identical carriers. **But a registration-instant repair LANDS AN OP AND IS SAVED BY NOTHING**: the package's save producers are a `file`-tier write's commit and a restore that moved (`guide §9.3`), so a profile whose only act is the boot keeps the **zero-length** file `guide §9.1` declares and the next boot performs the import again — measured by rewriting the carrier between the two boots and reading the NEW row back, the atime instrument being dead on this volume. So the import is read **once per boot until the first save, and once per profile after it**; the answers are identical either way, because both carriers are read-only inputs this module never writes, and **the file's size after a boot is `0` — the same figure `D-84` carried — so `S-1`, `S-4`, `S-8`, `S-10`, `S-11`, `S-12`, both `isolation` rows and `SC-B-01`/`SC-B-03` are all UNMOVED**, checked one by one rather than inferred from the name set.

**WHAT THE READ PATH LOSES, AND THE ONE ARM THAT KEEPS A DEFAULT.** `overlay`, `appImport` and `NO_IMPORT`'s served-array role are gone: `read()` is `plain(durable) ? fromStored(durable) : FIRST_RUN` and `readAppState()` reads the record's rows, the record's names and the construction's word. **The read-side defaults are NOT deleted**: `FIRST_RUN`, `SAFE_PAIR` and the empty row list answer the arm NO declaration can reach — **a refused construction has no instance, hence no batch and no repair** (`D-84`'s own measurement) — and `FIRST_RUN` is also the placeholder the record's repair writes, so the constant is one authority and not two. **The journal moves and nothing asserts it absolutely except the row that measures it**: the boot's three repairs are the journal's first three ops, so the first caller-visible ordinal is `4` where `D-84` measured `2` and the head before it `1`; `CD-5` pins it, and the e2e rows assert the ordinal only as an integer.

**THE ROWS, EACH RED ON REVERT.** `tests/unit/security-store-storeroom/creation-declarations.test.ts` is re-derived (`CD-1`'s census, `CD-3`, `CD-4`'s by-address body lookup, `CD-5`'s ordinal, `CD-6`'s now-built census) and gains `CD-7`…`CD-12` — the record's creation instant imported-or-placeholder, the row list's with the word in it, the import reaching the WRITTEN record off the bytes, two boots by op count with the unsaved limit pinned, the corrupt arms, and the refused construction. With the two declarations removed from `declarationsFor`, **10 of the 12 red** (`expected [] to deeply equal [['operator-settings',…]]`, `cursor: 2` vs `4`, `expected undefined to deeply equal {…4}`, `legacy:'absent'` where `'corrupt'` stands) and green restored. `tests/unit/security-store-storeroom/read-path-census.test.ts` is new and gating: `RW-1` asserts the two verbs call neither arm, hold no `readFileSync` and name no carrier — **over code with comments elided, its positive control being that the raw bytes DO carry both names** (`importOnce` **5** raw / **2** elided, `importAppStateOnce` **4** / **2**) — and `RW-2` pins that census, so the hollow-green class cannot pass: restoring a read-side import in `readAppState` reds `RW-1` on the ELIDED text while the prose still names the arm. `tests/integration/secure-panels/persistence.test.ts`'s five byte rows are re-derived, never weakened: each image is now the WHOLE file, naming the `['operator-app-state']` record the boot writes beside `['operator-settings']` (measured: `{"operator-app-state":{"legacy":"absent","rows":[],"unrepresentable":[]},"operator-settings":{…}}`).

**IT CLOSES `D-84`'s STOPPED HALF AND `D-82`'s `V1` AT ALL THREE ADDRESSES**, which is the clause-1 deviation that row recorded: every address of the secure crossing is an enforced invariant repaired at creation, and boot data comes from a file or from a constraint repair everywhere. `D-84`'s stopped paragraph is read through this row **BY POINTER AND NEVER REWORDED** (`D-46`), and `strata/pending.md` row 5 leaves that file with its reason recorded there. Its register is D-71 to D-84: the change push returned to the record root, a repair's credit made explicit, a repair write made to name its commit, the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero, the landing entry's id decided, the landing seat derived and that id moved, the renderer client's Stage A, the two-crossing ruling with its purge, the surplus instance collapsed, the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store, the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated, the data-flow convention audited clause by clause, the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration, and the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape.

**⟶ AND THE IDEMPOTENCE SECTION ABOVE IS RE-MEASURED UNDER CHALLENGE, AND IT STANDS — READ THROUGH THIS CLAUSE AND NEVER REWORDED (`D-46`).** **The challenge, the user, verbatim:** *"It's a file-tier write, so it saves to file after constraints pass. This provides durability"*. **IT DOES NOT, AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT SETTLES IT IS NOT A FILE'S SIZE BUT A SECOND INSTANCE OVER THE SAME `filePath`** — a store constructed with **no** constraint list and so with **no** repair, whose answer is therefore the DISK's own (`guide §9.1`: *"a fresh construction at the same `filePath` re-boots from those bytes"*). **Measured 2026-11-03, one fresh process per arm, over `provident-storeroom@1.9.0`:** a repair at a registration instant writes `{seeded:'from-the-repair'}` through the route, the constructing instance answers it, and **a second instance over the same bytes answers `AddressNotFound` and a register of `{}`** — with `size 0`, empty bytes and the construction's own inode, unchanged after a 200 ms tick; **the same profile after ONE caller `file`-tier write** saves the whole register image (`{"a-caller-address":{"by":"the-caller"},"seeded":{"seeded":"from-the-repair"}}`, `78` bytes, a NEW inode) and the second instance then answers the repaired value — one instrument, the opposite answer, one variable; and the arm where the pair **ACCEPTS** what the repair wrote, so that the pass genuinely passes, leaves the same `0` bytes. The reading is identical at **both** population routes, `cfg.constraints` and `addConstraint`, and at the app's own path (`createOperatorSettings` over a seeded profile: `0` bytes after construction, and the second instance's first caller write at `cursor: 4` — three repairs again). **THE MECHANISM, WHICH IS WHY "AFTER CONSTRAINTS PASS" DOES NOT REACH THE FILE:** **the save is the TERMINAL ARM's act** — `guide §9.2`'s *"the terminal arm performs the store's `commit_file` as its FIRST act on the PASSING arm"* and `index.d.ts:631`'s *"the save is the ARM's, at the record's own op"* — **and a registration performs no terminal act at all**: `guide §7.2` (4)'s *"**THE WRITE RUNS NO PASS OF ITS OWN**"* read with its binding's own home, *"it appends no `constraint-check` entry, no `outcome` entry and **no terminal act**, delivers no listener, and opens no attempt"* (`src/entry/kernel-registration.mjs:487`). So the repair's write is a `file`-tier write whose commit is the REGISTRATION's, and the registration has no terminal arm to save it; the earlier citation of `§9.3` named the PRODUCERS of the bytes and never their instant, which is what this clause supplies. **`CD-10` IS RE-DERIVED TO THIS INSTRUMENT AND NOT TO A SIZE**: it asserts, on one side, that a second instance answers BOTH records once a caller's save has run, and on the other that it answers `AddressNotFound` at both `file` addresses until then — and **every zero-length claim the limit carried** (`S-1` and `S-4` of `settings-carrier-retirement`, `S-1` of `security-store-storeroom`, `SC-B-03`, both `isolation` rows, which assert no size at all, and `persistence.md`/`design.md`/`interface.md`'s "the boot writes no byte") **is re-measured rather than inferred.**

**⟶ AND THIS CLAUSE'S READING IS OVERTURNED BY `D-86`, READ THROUGH IT AND NEVER REWORDED (`D-46`).** **`provident-storeroom@1.9.1`, installed 2026-11-03, PUTS THE SAVE AT THE LANDING ARM**: a registration-instant repair's `file`-tier write is in the file when the constructor returns, at the construction's own inode, and a second store over the same `filePath` with no constraint list answers the repaired value — the same instrument this clause ran, one release later, with the opposite answer. So the idempotence section above and this clause are read through `D-86`: the import runs **once per profile** and not once per boot, the file's size after a boot is `125` over a cold profile and `210` over a carrier, `CD-10` is re-derived onto the durability arm, and every zero-length claimant this clause enumerates is re-measured there rather than inferred from the name set. **No byte of the text above moves.**

## D-86 — `provident-storeroom` `1.9.1` PUTS THE SAVE AT THE LANDING ARM: a registration-instant repair's `file` write IS in the file, `D-85`'s durability limit is FALSE by measurement, and every zero-length claimant is re-derived to the state the product has

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"The user says `1.9.1` is now published"*, with the five-part pass it asked for; plus the measurements below, all driven 2026-11-03 on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1` (published 07:28Z, read at a scratch cache because the default one is root-owned and dies `EACCES`).

**THE GATE, THEN THE INSTALL, AND BOTH MEASURED RATHER THAN TRUSTED.** `npm view` answered `1.9.1` at the version, at `dist-tags` and at the tail of the version list before a byte was moved. `npm install provident-storeroom@1.9.1` moved the **eleven cells** together — the main manifest's `version`, its five exact `optionalDependencies` pins and the five platform manifests' versions — and the lock's **six nodes** carry `1.9.1` with fresh `resolved`/`integrity` (the main node plus the five `@auspicion/provident-storeroom-js-*`). **AND THE TARGETED INSTALL PRUNED THE INSTALLED LINUX BINDING, WHICH A PLAIN `npm install` RESTORED** — measured: after `npm install <pkg>@1.9.1` no platform package stood in `node_modules` and the store answered `FilePathUnreachable` naming its own binding, while `npm install` from the lock put all five entries back and the store constructed; **a consumer hazard, recorded and never worked around**.

**THE SURFACE THE RELEASE SAYS IT DOES NOT MOVE, RE-MEASURED — AND ONE FIGURE MOVED.** Fourteen own string members in the same order (`read` · `readLiteral` · `write` · `delete` · `rollback` · `listen` · `unlisten` · `addConstraint` · `constraintOn` · `constraintOff` · `constraints` · `removeListener` · `removeConstraint` · `register`), the closed status set at **twenty** ranked `1`…`20` with `(string & {})` its compile-time escape and `RollbackCompacted` still the eleventh, and `Tier` still `'mem' | 'file' | 'temp'` (`index.d.ts:179`, `:358`-`:381`). **The own-symbol count is what moved: `1.9.0` read 12 at construction and this tree reads 13**, the thirteenth being `Symbol(provident-storeroom.registration-save)` and sitting fifth in `getOwnPropertySymbols`' order; after one `write` the figure is 14 where `1.9.0` read 13, the store's own `Symbol(…kernel)` growth being the same on both trees (measured 2026-11-03, one fresh instance per tree). `STOREROOM_DECLARED_SURFACE.symbolCount` therefore reads `13`, `tests/e2e/central-data/scenarios.test.ts` `S-9` asserts it, and every live pin moved together: `package.json`, `tests/storeroom-surface.test.ts` `S-1.3`, `strata/brief.md` §2's packages row and its seam row, `src/main/storeroom.ts`'s census header, `docs/L3/central-data/design.md` §2's symbol clause. **Every dated reading stays exactly as written**: `grep -rn "1\.9\.0\|1\.8\.0\|1\.7\.0"` over `src/`, `tests/`, `docs/` and `strata/` answers **55 sites** beside this file's own 26 lines, and every one of them is a measurement of its own install read through this row; **the SIX lines that stated a LIVE figure are the ones moved** (the two manifests' pin sites, `brief.md` §2, `src/main/storeroom.ts`'s header and `symbolCount` docblock, and `docs/L3/central-data/design.md` §2's symbol clause), and no dated byte moved.

**THE FIVE ACTS, EACH QUOTED FROM WHERE THE RELEASE PUT IT.** `RELEASE-NOTES.md` §1.9.1 item 1, verbatim: *"A REVERSAL'S SAVE — THE FILE HOLDS THE STATE THE REVERSAL RESTORED, WRITTEN AFTER THE JUDGEMENT THAT JUDGED IT"*; item 2: *"A LISTENER A REVERSAL'S REPAIR FIRES IS INVOKED ONCE, WITH THE TRIGGERING ADDRESS, BEFORE `rollback` RETURNS"*; item 3: *"A REVERSAL'S JUDGEMENT RUNS YOUR REPAIRS, AND THE CREDIT IS THE FAILING PAIR'S"*; item 4: *"A REFUSING REGISTRATION NOW ROLLS BACK — NO LANDED EDIT SURVIVES A REFUSAL, WITHOUT EXCEPTION"*; item 5: *"A `filePath` WHOSE BYTES ARE NOT THE STORE'S IMAGE NO LONGER REFUSES — A REVERSAL REBUILDS FROM THE CREATION STATE AND THE NEXT SAVE WRITES A READABLE IMAGE"*. **THE DECLARATION MOVED AT TWO CLAUSE BLOCKS AND NOWHERE ELSE** (`diff` of the two installed `index.d.ts`, 24 changed lines): items 1–3 ride ONE block, `index.d.ts:959`-`:966`, *"…the listeners the repair's write fires are invoked ONCE PER DISTINCT HANDLE with the TRIGGERING ADDRESS as their single argument, AFTER the reversal's save and BEFORE this member returns … and a judgement that refuses, a pair body that throws, and a reversal that fired nothing all invoke NOTHING"*; and the save's own carrier sentence is the delete clause re-derived at `:628`-`:637`, *"THE SAVE IS KEYED TO WHAT THE ACT MOVED AND NOT TO THE OP'S OWN TIER WORD"*. **ITEMS 4 AND 5 HAVE NO DECLARATION CLAUSE AT ALL** — `StoreStatus` is unmoved at twenty, `storeCreate`'s and `addConstraint`'s docblocks are byte-identical, and the release states both as behaviour only; that asymmetry is recorded and not read as a defect.

**THE DURABILITY LIMIT IS FALSE, AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT SETTLES IT IS `D-85`'S OWN.** A store constructed with ONE creation declaration whose repair writes `{seeded: 'from-the-repair'}` through the registration's three-position route: **the file stands at 39 bytes — `{"seeded":{"seeded":"from-the-repair"}}` — when the constructor returns, at the construction's own inode, unchanged after a 200 ms tick, and a SECOND store over that `filePath` with NO constraint list and therefore NO repair ANSWERS THE REPAIRED VALUE** (measured 2026-11-03, one fresh process per arm). The same reading at **both** population routes (`cfg.constraints` and `addConstraint`), each 39 bytes and each answered by the second instance. **At the app's own two `file` addresses, through the real `createOperatorSettings` over a seeded profile: `['operator-settings']` and `['operator-app-state']` are BOTH on the disk at 198–283 bytes when the constructor returns, the second instance answers both, the inode does not move, and `['operator-exclusion']` alone answers `AddressNotFound` — because `mem` can never come from a file (`D-84`'s own rule, unmoved).** **TWO-BOOT IDEMPOTENCE, ACROSS TWO PROCESSES**: boot one imports `tok-1` and the carrier's two rows and saves both; the retired carriers are then REWRITTEN (`tok-2`, `right`); **boot two answers boot one's `tok-1` and `left`, the file is byte-identical at 283 bytes and the same inode, and not one byte of either rewritten carrier reached it** — the import did not re-run, because the record it wrote is the marker. The figure `1.9.1` replaces is exact: `D-85` measured `AddressNotFound`, `size 0`, empty bytes at this same instrument over `1.9.0`, and its mechanism — *"the save is the TERMINAL ARM's act and a registration performs no terminal act"* — is now false at the member that matters, because **the landing arm is the repair's own write and the save rides it**.

**THE ROWS AND THE DOCUMENTS, EACH RE-DERIVED TO THE PRODUCT AND NONE WEAKENED — NINETEEN CLAUSES OVER THIRTEEN TEST FILES BESIDE THE LIVE DRIVER, FIFTEEN OVER SIXTEEN CORPUS FILES, ONE ROW ADDED.** The suite's red set after the install was **20 of 3,109 over 125 files**; two are the worktree boundary rows (red by construction on a dirty tree: `store-tabs-record.test.ts`'s `REG-EXEC` and `S-1`) and one was the pin. The seventeen: `CD-1`/`CD-3`/`CD-10`/`CD-11` (`cursor` `4`→`2` on a second boot, and `CD-10` re-derived onto the durability instrument above), `instance.test.ts`'s three, `operator-settings.test.ts`'s one at unit grain and `OS-7`/`OS-8`, `AS-1`, `IMP-1`/`IMP-2`, `S-1` of both e2e scenario files, `S-4` of `settings-carrier-retirement`, `secure-panels`' `B-1`/`B-4`, and the two BARE-STORE rows whose assertions stood but whose premise did not (`central-data/surface.test.ts`'s first row and `tests/store-compliance-live.mjs`'s record reader) — **every one re-derived to the whole file image the product now writes** (`125` cold, `140` seeded, `158`/`187`/`198`/`210`/`223`/`283` per arm, each asserted as the file's own text and never as a length alone), and the deaths named in place: *"the boot is read-only"*, *"the import writes no byte"*, *"zero-length until the first save"*, *"a second construction imports again"*. `OS-7`'s dead half is the sharpest and is named so: the import now runs **once per profile**, and the row's own second construction answers the saved record. **AND ONE ROW IS ADDED, because the batteries had been green for the wrong reason**: the central-data profiles are stood by `createProfileStore({ filePath })` with NO constraint list, while the app's boot batches six tab-record declarations and `tabs-must-stand` acts at the registration instant — so `tests/integration/central-data/profile-file.test.ts` gains *THE APP'S OWN BOOT* (`160` bytes, the landing record, the boot the only writer) and its bare-store premise is stated as such. Documents: `docs/L3/security-store-storeroom/design.md` §4, `docs/L2/security-store-storeroom/{persistence,interface,behaviour}.md`, `docs/L2/settings-carrier-retirement/persistence.md` §2/§4, `docs/L2/secure-panels/persistence.md` §2-§4/§8, `docs/L2/central-data/persistence.md` §1, `docs/L3/central-data/design.md` §2, `docs/L1/security-store-storeroom.md`, `docs/L1/security-store-storeroom-scenarios.md` `SC-E5-01`, `docs/L1/settings-carrier-retirement.md`, `docs/L1/central-data-management.md`, and the three unit records. **Ceilings held by trading lines**: `store-tabs-record-unit.md` 40, `pending.md` 39, L3 security `design.md` 149, and both `interface.md` and `docs/L1/security-store-storeroom.md` at exactly 150.

**THE THREE OTHER BEHAVIOURS, EACH DRIVEN.** **(i) A REFUSAL NOW ROLLS BACK, AND THE WRITE THAT STOOD BEFORE A REFUSAL NO LONGER DOES** — measured at all three instants: a `cfg.constraints` registration whose repair writes and whose re-run refuses leaves `size 0`, empty bytes and a second instance answering `AddressNotFound` and `{}`; an `addConstraint` over lawful bytes leaves `[x]` absent, `[anchor]` intact, the register `{anchor:{a:1}}` and the bytes byte-identical; and a caller write whose pass refuses answers `ConstraintPassRefused` with the address still at its pre-write value and the bytes unmoved. **The old reading lives in no row and no document of this tree** — `grep -rn "composes no rollback\|STANDS, because a registration"` answers nothing — and the one place it did live, `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` line 20's *"the repair's own write stands at `b`"*, is read through this row by pointer and corrected in place. **(ii) A NON-IMAGE `filePath` NO LONGER REFUSES**: driven with `this is not the store's image\n`, the construction answers (no throw), the register is `{}`, the next write commits and the bytes are replaced; and a `rollback` over bytes that stopped being the store's image answers a `RollbackRecord` and rewrites `{"alpha":"one"}` — the creation-state rebuild of item 5 — where the row `profile-file.test.ts` drives still holds as filed. **(iii) `RollbackCompacted` IS UNRAISABLE**: `grep -rn "RollbackCompacted" src/ tests/ docs/ strata/` answers **one** live hit — `docs/L3/central-data/signatures.md:124`'s status-CENSUS row, which stays correct because the status keeps its place in the twenty — and every other hit is in `archive/` (the retired gate corpus) or in this row's own quotation; no row anywhere asserts its reachability, so nothing is retired and nothing is re-derived.

**THE FOUR CHECKS THE RELEASE ASKS OF A CONSUMER, ANSWERED WITH EVIDENCE AND NO CODE CHANGE NEEDED.** **A rollback leaving the file alone**: `grep -rn "\.rollback(" src/` answers **zero** — this app issues no reversal, so item 1's save has no producer here and nothing can be left un-saved by it. **Listener bodies that write**: exactly two `register` sites, `src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:854` (whose body is `try { listener(detach(address)) } catch { withdraw(standing, handles) }`) and `src/main/tabs-record/index.ts:1248` (whose body is `try { handler() } catch { /* contained */ }`) — **neither writes**: the delivery hands a subscriber the address, and no body of this tree issues a store verb, so item 2's delivery can invoke nothing that writes during a rollback. **Repairs relying on standing after a refusal**: none — the three declarations' repairs write only at a registration instant and answer through `repairAnswer`, and where the re-run refused, the store rolled the landing back (measured above), so a repair that depended on its edit surviving would now be wrong **and none does**; no comment, row or document of this tree claims one. **Any `filePath` not exclusively owned**: the app opens **two** store files — `<userData>/provident-operator-settings.json` (`src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:706`) and `<userData>/provident-profile.json` (`src/main/main.ts:156`/`:160`) — each written only by its own store and read besides by `src/main/data/store.ts`'s name reading (`lstat`/`stat`/`access`, no byte, ahead of any instance), the tab record constructing nothing and importing the package for types alone; **beyond that nothing in this process opens either path, and the only other writers possible are a second app instance on the same `userData` and the operator**, which no line of this tree can exclude.

**THE FIGURES, AND THE ROWS THAT ARE RED BY CONSTRUCTION NAMED RATHER THAN COUNTED AWAY.** On the dirty tree the suite read **4 failed | 3,104 passed | 2 skipped of 3,110 over 125 files**, and **every one of the four is a worktree-boundary row that reads the pass's own diff** — `store-tabs-record.test.ts`'s `REG-EXEC` and `S-1`, `gesture-session.test.ts`'s `F-9(b)` and `store-core-graph.test.ts`'s `R-9` (the last two name `src/main/storeroom.ts` and `package.json` themselves) — so the four legs are run and reported on the committed tree, where they are **0/0/0/0** and the count is **3,108 passed / 2 skipped of 3,110**, one row more than `D-85`'s `3,109` for the row this pass adds. **AND ONE PACKAGE FINDING IS DISCHARGED RATHER THAN CARRIED**: `P-1` of `docs/L2/security-store-storeroom/persistence.md` §9 and `SS-F1` of `strata/records/security-store-storeroom-unit.md` — *"a boot over bytes that are not the store's image has no declared answer"* — is answered by item 5, measured, and both rows say so in place while staying as the reading of their own date.

Its register is D-71 to D-85: the change push returned to the record root, a repair's credit made explicit, a repair write made to name its commit, the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero, the landing entry's id decided, the landing seat derived and that id moved, the renderer client's Stage A, the two-crossing ruling with its purge, the surplus instance collapsed, the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store, the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated, the data-flow convention audited clause by clause, the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration, the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation, and the absence-keyed import put inside the repair. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own decision ids — the five rows its §1.9.1 cites (the op-free repair credit, the save arm keyed to what an act moved, the unconditional refusal rollback, and the reversal's save and delivery) — are the PACKAGE's corpus's and are never spelled with a bare number here, because this file's numbering is this repository's, and a defect of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction and proposed fix shape.

## D-87 — THE PURGE'S LAST DELETION GETS ITS TWO SUCCESSOR ADDRESSES: the pane geometry at `['panes']` and the focus mirror at `['focus']`, two roots on the NORMAL crossing, and the frozen store is NOT deleted here

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, verbatim — *"The purge's last deletion is the app's own frozen graph store … It cannot go until two data sets have a successor address … Build them."* — plus the measurements below, all driven 2026-11-03 on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN, AND IT DECIDES THE PASS.** **The pane geometry** is `file.settings.pane.<id>.size`/`.bounds` beside `mem.layout.pane.<id>.size`/`.bounds`: `readPaneSize`/`readPaneBounds` (`src/renderer/renderer.ts`, the `startSizeOf`/`boundsOf` closures) read **`mem` FIRST and `file` second**, and the release turn writes the SAME `final` to both tiers, `commit('file.settings.pane.pane-a.size', final)` then `commit('mem.layout.pane.pane-a.size', final)`. **There is ONE pane id under `src/` and it is not authored**: `paneIdOf` derives it from the caller's element or token and falls back to **`pane-a`**, which the release turn also hard-codes — while the authored DOM pane is a different string (`demo-envelope.ts` authors `gutter-pane`), so the address is keyed by the id the COMPOSITION addresses. **The default geometry is the tree's own and was measured, not invented**: the authored pane's `props` read `size '100'`, `min '0'`, `max '200'`, and `gutterSeamExample`'s `startSizeOf`/`boundsOf` fall back to the same three figures; **the frozen store holds NO pane default at all** (`grep -n 'pane' src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts` → 0), so a read that missed at both tiers answered `undefined` — an unusable value that made the release refuse `'unusable-default'` with ZERO writes. **The clamp is one site and one rule** (`src/shared/gutter.ts`'s `clampToBounds`): a non-number, or a pair whose `min`/`max` is not a number, answers `NaN`, and `NaN` makes the move INVALID; the release applies it only to a **standing `mem` value**, against `readPaneBounds({}, 'pane-a')`. **The focus mirror** is `mem.focus.entries` (`readonly FocusEntry[]`, each `{id, target, label?}` with `id` and `target` opaque) and `mem.focus.activeId` (`FocusId | null`): `mirrorStateOf` reads both, `answerForCarrier` commits both through the model's `persist` seam at one instant per reference, `createFocusCarrier`'s `persistTarget` is the same pair, and the mirror is CLEARED at the carrier's boot mint (`commit('mem.focus', undefined)` then `clear('mem.focus')` where the root is cold). **`focusCarrier` holds NO state** — it holds the STORE HANDLE, and its own docblock says the state lives in the `mem.focus.*` mirror and never in a module-level carrier.

**THE CROSSING IS THE NORMAL ONE, DECIDED ON EVIDENCE AND NOT PREFERENCE.** Both families are UI state with no security character — the tab record's own ground — while the secure crossing is the operator's security record and a resize or a focus transition is no authority act; and **the decisive finding is that the SECURE crossing's legacy rows address holds the frozen store's `file.*` projection of these very rows** (D-78's item (f)), so putting the new addresses there would stand the new value and its legacy projection inside ONE store, and the purge would be deleting from inside the operator's record. On the NORMAL crossing the legacy projection stays wholly in the secure store and every address this pass creates is wholly in the normal one, so **the purge deletes the projection and its rows and touches no address created here**. The cost, stated rather than hidden: until the purge, the same fact stands in two stores — a stale `file.settings.pane.*` row written by `Y-2`'s `STORE_FILE_PUT` and a fresh `['panes']` value — and nothing reconciles them.

**THE ADDRESS SHAPE IS TWO ROOTS, `['panes']` (`file`) AND `['focus']` (`mem`), AND THE TIER DECIDED IT.** The tab record's pattern is one root with members; it was followed until the measurement forbade it: **a nested write does not create its parent** (`write(['view','panes'], 'file', …)` over a fresh store answers `AddressNotFound: 'view' does not exist in ''`), so a one-root shape needs a THIRD declaration minting the parent branch, whose own body is handed `undefined` at the registration instant; and **a root written at `file` answers its descendants at `file` and `TierNotFound` at `mem`**, so one value could not carry the `mem` family at all. So the pattern is applied **once per tier** rather than once per family: each family is ONE WHOLE VALUE at its own root, under its own existence declaration.

**THE DECLARATIONS — TWO, BATCHED INTO THE BOOT'S ONE `createProfileStore` CALL BESIDE THE TAB RECORD'S SIX (D-65).** `panes-must-stand` at `['panes']` refuses a value that is not a plain record and repairs it to the measured default family; `focus-must-stand` at `['focus']` refuses the same arm and repairs it to the declared empty pair. **Both bodies judge `context.value` and neither is wrapped by the null-store guard** — `tabsMustStand`'s shape and its measured reason (the boot binds its holder only after `createProfileStore` returns) — and **both repairs write ONE whole legal value through the module's one repair-write path**, which branches on the record's own `write` member: three positions at a registration instant, and `writeJoinedAt`'s required carrier at `write`'s FOURTH at a landing one, every arm answering through `repairAnswer` (D-72, D-73). **`focus-must-stand` is `operator-exclusion-must-stand`'s shape exactly** and for its reason: a `mem` value can never come from a file (D-84), so a repair at the creation instant is its only lawful source. Files, each the tab record's counterpart: `src/main/view-record/index.ts`, `src/main/view-surface/index.ts`, `src/shared/view-channels.ts` (four constants), and the bridge member `view` declared in lockstep in `src/main/preload.ts` and `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts`.

**THE ACCEPTANCE BAR, DRIVEN PER ADDRESS AND READ OFF THE ROWS.** *(1) Existence + placeholder at the creation instant*: with the batch supplied and the address absent, the store's OWN read answers `{'pane-a': {size: 100, bounds: {min: 0, max: 200}}}` and `{entries: []}` before any route of the module runs, while a store carrying no batch answers `AddressNotFound` at the same address; **a fresh profile therefore answers the default geometry and the mirror at its default, both STANDING** — `read().source` reads `{panes:'record', focus:'record'}` — and a seeded family stands byte-identical (the pair is not a rewrite). *(2) The listener*: one delivery per committed change of EITHER family (1, then 2, then 3 over two pane commits and a focus commit), **ZERO** for a write at `['elsewhere']`, and the surface's `attach` turns the one subscription into the broadcast while the preload registers the same constant and does not re-spell it. *(3) Boot data*: two successive boots answer the same state, the profile carries `["panes"]` and **no byte of the `mem` family**, and boot two is handed `['focus']` **ABSENT** at the pair's own context and its repair mints it again — clause 3 answered for a family a file can never carry. *(4) Writes*: four paths and no fifth — `commitPane` and `commitFocus` (IPC routes, UI acts) and the two creation repairs — with **a read writing nothing, measured both ways** (the file's bytes unmoved over a standing family, and an absent `mem` family still absent after three reads) and no timer, interval or poll in the module or its surface. **Eight reverts were driven and each answered RED then restored**: either pair out of the batch (4 and 3 rows), the repair's answer widened to admit `void` (TS2578 twice, `npm run typecheck` over `src/**` staying CLEAN — the leg that catches it is `typecheck:tests`), the carrier position made optional (TS2578, and `src/**` still clean), the landing arm writing without naming the commit (the landing row), a read that repairs (the no-write-on-read row), the bridge half-widened in the renderer alone (the lockstep row), and the view record out of the boot's batch (the batch row).

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED.** The boot's profile image gains a root — `160 → 221` bytes over a fresh profile, `{"panes":…,"tabs":…}` — so **the declared bridge member set moves EIGHT → NINE in four censuses** (`tests/store-persist.test.ts`'s `DECLARED_TOP_LEVEL_BRIDGE_MEMBERS`, both `isolation.test.ts` files' `BRIDGE_MEMBERS`, and the settings-carrier scenario's ISO-4 list), each keeping its set assertion; `tests/integration/central-data/profile-file.test.ts`'s *THE APP'S OWN BOOT* is re-derived from one root to two, and it now batches the entry's own EIGHT declarations rather than six. **`tests/store-core-graph.test.ts`'s R-11 was re-derived rather than dodged**: its subject is the frozen paths' VALUE-SURFACE IMPORTERS, and it read raw `git grep` lines, so every later purge row's docblock naming the store it succeeds would red it — it now judges each candidate FILE's comment-elided code with that file's own `stripComments`, keeps the raw census as its subject list, and carries a two-arm positive control (a code mention is still a hit, the same mention in prose is not). Three other rows were saved by rewording this pass's own prose and not by touching them — `S-9`'s importing-FILE census and the settings-carrier ISO-7 address census are raw-byte reads, so this module names the package and the secure crossing's address without spelling either import or address. **`npm test` is `3,128 passed / 2 skipped` of `3,130` over `126` files** (`3,108` of `3,110` before this pass, so the `20` rows are this battery's), `srcTypeScriptFiles` `49 → 52`, `mapRows` `21 → 22`, and the marks `126` files with gating `134 → 154` sites in `22 → 23` files, REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` and never hand-edited; the four legs are **0/0/0/0**, and the boundary rows observed on the dirty tree were **two rows in each of TWO files** (`store-tabs-record.test.ts`'s `S-1` and `REG-EXEC`, `store-core-graph.test.ts`'s `R-9` and `I-11`) beside one in each of two more.

**THE FROZEN STORE IS NOT DELETED HERE, AND THAT IS THIS PASS'S ONE EXPLICIT LIMIT.** `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts` (2,372 lines) stands, `src/renderer/renderer.ts`'s write paths are untouched, and the renderer is wired to these addresses NOWHERE: the swap is the NEXT pass, in the order the tab record's own sequence ran — crossing, then client, then purge. **Three facts the next pass inherits**: the pane geometry's `mem`/`file` precedence COLLAPSES to one `file` address (the `mem` copy is the in-gesture transient's), `activeId` is written as ABSENCE because `null` is refused at the crossing's boundary, and the legacy `file.*` rows keep being written by `Y-2` with nothing reading them until the purge deletes that arm.

**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46). Its register is D-71 to D-86: the change push returned to the record root, a repair's credit made explicit, a repair write made to name its commit, the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero, the landing entry's id decided, the landing seat derived and that id moved, the renderer client's Stage A, the two-crossing ruling with its purge, the surplus instance collapsed, the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store, the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated, the data-flow convention audited clause by clause, the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration, the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation, the absence-keyed import put inside the repair, and `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (D-5, D-52).

## D-88 — THE RENDERER SWAP IS SOLVED IN DESIGN AND **STOPPED ON MEASUREMENT**: the shape that answers the async crossing, the timing it changes per call site, and the marked surface that pins the machinery it replaces — nothing moved, nothing deleted, and the tree is the head's

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, verbatim — *"This pass points the renderer at them"*, *"the data shape is driven by the listener events"* — and the measurement below, driven 2026-11-03 on `c8e8689` over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`. **It is a measurement and a REFUSAL, which is the shape `D-78`'s item (f) stage took**, and it changes nothing in the tree: `git status --short` is empty at it.

**THE CRUX, AND THE SHAPE THAT ANSWERS IT.** The frozen store is SYNCHRONOUS AND IN-REALM; the crossing is IPC AND ASYNCHRONOUS. The synchronous callers are the pane-drag reads (`renderer.ts:1700-1722`), the `release` write (`:1823-1825`), the focus carrier's reads and commits (`:1104-1105`, `:1152-1153`, `:1967-1968`, `:1936-1937`) and the tab machinery. **THE USER'S CONVENTION IS THE SOLUTION, AND IT IS BUILT AS THE `tabs-client` PRECEDENT IS**: a RENDERER-SIDE CLIENT holds the data shape, is refreshed by `onChanged` deliveries, is read SYNCHRONOUSLY by the drag and focus paths, and writes through the crossing's routes. **THAT SHAPE IS NOT A STORE** — `D-78`'s ruling 3 and `D-82`'s clause 3: it holds **no address space** (a caller names a pane id, never a path and never a tier), it **owns no persistence** and opens no file, it **carries no constraint, no repair, no journal, no listener registration and no instance**, and it **is thrown away and refilled WHOLE by every delivery** — a store survives a restart by design and this survives exactly one delivery, which is the whole of why it can never be used as one and must never be named one.

**BUILT, TYPECHECKED AND DRIVEN, THEN REVERTED: WHAT THE SWAP WOULD HAVE DONE, WITH ITS TIMING AT EACH SITE.** `src/renderer/view-client.ts` was written (457 lines) with `src/shared/view-shape.ts` (85) carrying the two families' vocabulary and the measured defaults so both realms read one spelling; `typecheck` and `typecheck:tests` were 0 over both. *(1) READS* — `readPaneSize`/`readPaneBounds` answer `paneGeometryIn(shape.panes, id)` in the CALLER'S OWN TURN, the `mem`/`file` precedence collapsing to ONE `file` address (D-87's inherited fact (i)) and a pane the family does not name answering the MEASURED DEFAULT — 100 and `{min:0,max:200}` — instead of the `undefined` that made the release refuse `'unusable-default'` with zero writes; `mirrorStateOf` answers the shape's `{entries, activeId}` with the absent active read back as `null` (the crossing writes absence, D-87's fact (ii)); the tab record's reads answer the tab client's held reading. *(2) WRITES* — `release` sends ONE `commitPane(id, {size})` where it committed the same `final` to TWO tiers; it returns `void`, which it always returned, and the SINK is still invoked BEFORE the route is sent, so **no caller depends on the old timing** and the receipt lands in the client's `writeOutcome()` a tick later. The focus carrier sends ONE `commitFocus(wholeMirror)` where it committed two references, and its seam returns the client's `{status:'sent'}` statement rather than the store's receipt by identity — **no caller reads that receipt** (`persist`'s `value` member was always discarded), and the ROUTE'S ANSWER is the MODEL's next state, composed before the write, so a delivery arriving a tick later moves the SHAPE and never the answer. A mirror the crossing's boundary refuses (an id that is not a string, number or boolean) is recorded as a refusal where the frozen store would have taken it: a real behaviour change, stated. *(3) THE BOOT READ* — `startTabsClientInstrument`'s held reading becomes the TAB shape's first fill and the view client's `fill()` sits at the same last line of `main()`, beside it: **ONE STATED BOOT READ, then deliveries**, because a boot's own store acts and repair passes happened BEFORE this realm could subscribe and a cold profile changes nothing afterwards — so the first fill has no delivery to ride — and every later fill is a delivery's, with no timer, no poll and no write on any read. *(4) THE TAB MACHINERY* — `readTabsRef` is answered by the client's reading, `mintTabId` by its `order`, `closeTab` by the crossing's `remove`, and `driveTabsLandingPage` by the tabs client's OWN witness derivation, whose answer `main()` discards today (D-78's measurement). **`TABS_CONSTRAINT` LEAVES THE RENDERER** because the crossing declares `exactly-one-active` (`src/main/tabs-record/index.ts`, `D-83`'s batch), so the frozen store's construction is handed **NO `constraints` member at all** — nothing at all rather than an empty list, because an empty list is still a declaration that a constraint set exists and `buildWiredGraphStore`'s member becomes a name with nothing behind it.

**WHY NONE OF IT IS TAKEN: THE MARKED SURFACE IS A REWORK OF ITS OWN, MEASURED ROW BY ROW.** With the two families swapped and the tests untouched, `tests/pane-drag-compliance.test.ts` read **28 failed of 129** and `tests/store-focus.test.ts` **49 of 96**; giving each file's fixture a CROSSING DOUBLE — one client over a projected store, the delivery modelled at the write — took store-focus to **32**, and minting the `mem` mirror inside that double, as the crossing's creation repair does, took it to **20**. **THE RESIDUAL IS NOT A STALE EXPECTATION A PASS MAY RE-POINT**: in store-focus it is the file's `§2.4` registration/release family (P1, P3, P7, S24-1, SF-LEAK, F-3, M-5, R-4, P-2.6-4, both working defaults) together with the `§5.5.1` register's tally rows (P-SF-IM-2, P-SF-SM-1, P-SF-SM-2, P-SF-TP-1, P-SF-TP-2), whose declared terms COUNT the boot mint and the two `mem.focus.*` subscriptions — the two properties this swap deletes and the crossing's `focus-must-stand` plus the client's ONE subscription replace; in pane-drag it is the twelve `P-PD-IM-1` read rows, the `P-PD-SM-1`/`SM-3`/`TP-2` register rows and the write-census rows `F-5`/`F-6`/`M-5`, which count the old two-tier commit spelling. `tests/store-tabs-record.test.ts` was NOT attempted and is larger: its 100 rows pin the frozen store's tab region, 33 of them through `constrainedStore()`, and the invariant rows' successor is the crossing's own battery (`tests/integration/store-tabs-record/crossing.test.ts`) — so that file's disposition is `D-30`'s REPLACED-WHOLE and not a re-pointing.

**WHAT THE NEXT PASS OWNS, NAMED SO IT IS NOT REDESIGNED.** The `store-view-record` unit's swap pass takes the attempt as built (`/tmp/d88/attempt/` at this date: `view-client.ts` 457, `view-shape.ts` 85, the wiring and fixture diff 913 lines), **re-grains the two files' registers onto their subjects' new homes** — the crossing's `focus-must-stand` creation repair and the client's ONE subscription for the focus family, the client's shape and the route's call log for the panes family — deletes `tests/store-tabs-record.test.ts`'s rows WITH the region they pin, and carries the moved count into `brief.md` §6 in its own commit. **THE FROZEN STORE WAS NOT DELETED AND STANDING IS CORRECT**: `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts` (2,372 lines) is untouched, the renderer is wired to `['panes']` and `['focus']` NOWHERE, and D-87's three inherited facts — the collapsed precedence, `activeId` written as absence, and the legacy `file.*` rows standing in two stores unreconciled — are all still the next pass's.

**THE READINGS, ON THE RESTORED TREE, WHICH IS THE HEAD'S.** `npm test` **3,128 passed / 2 skipped of 3,130 over 126 files**, `typecheck` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `build` OK (five artifacts); the four Electron legs on `DISPLAY=:0`: `battery` **184 checks, 0 failures**, `divergence` green (**9 checks, 0 failures**, plus `4` extension checks), `ui` **11/11 assertions**, `panes` **7/7 rows** — each leg green with **zero survivors** after it and the process census unchanged around it. **The readings are the head's rather than a before/after pair because the pass left no byte behind**: there is no delta to attribute, and the pre-existing-versus-this-pass distinction is answered by the tree being `c8e8689` itself.

**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46). Its register is D-71 to D-87: the change push returned to the record root, a repair's credit made explicit, a repair write made to name its commit, the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero, the landing entry's id decided, the landing seat derived and that id moved, the renderer client's Stage A, the two-crossing ruling with its purge, the surplus instance collapsed, the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store, the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated, the data-flow convention audited clause by clause, the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration, the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation, the absence-keyed import put inside the repair, `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save, and the two successor addresses with the frozen store left standing. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, filed with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (D-5, D-52).
